- `74-MEMORY-LANE-INTEGRITY-PLAN.md` — **✅ DONE (build) 2026-08-13** on `develop-2.0-init` — all build phases shipped in seven commits (`e3b63cb` Phase 1 resolver, `fde6cfc` Phase 2 dead-path sweep, `ebb8f1f` Phase 3 falsifiable gate, `95cb64b` Phase 4 intake lane, `a45e162` OQ-5 install-clobber guard, `9a91998` Phase 5 disposition + maintainer gate, `ac2a1e0` Phase 6 docs); full suite 10727 passed, both maintainer gates PASS. **Phase 7 (consumer e2e) DEFERRED by the maintainer 2026-08-13 — the maintainer intends to run it later; it is NOT waived and NOT executed.** Read that precisely: the mechanism is **build-verified, NOT consumer-validated** — every phase closed behind its review loop, but nothing here has been exercised against a real consumer install, and deferring a check is not evidence it would pass. The highest-value item in the retained recipe is a known-answer regression anchor: re-run the motivating ticket through `/devforge:research` and confirm the memory entries reach evidence-gathering BEFORE scoping is committed (the correct outcome is already known from the second run of the original A/B). **Phase 0 RATIFIED 2026-08-13** (D1–D8 all CONFIRMED, OQ-1–OQ-6 all resolved, the disposition table ratified EXACTLY as drafted at 13 `READS` / 7 `N/A` with no row overridden, OQ-5 ratified IN SCOPE via the inline guard, OQ-6 resolved to DROP the field); only **Phase 7 (consumer e2e) remains, user-driven — NOT run.** The plan DOCUMENT is deliberately untracked (like 73 and 75, it carries private-client identifiers and this repo is PUBLIC), so the commit state is SPLIT: code committed, plan doc untracked. **All four mechanisms are closed.** (1) Dead path — `grep -rn "\.claude/memory" src/` returns ZERO and gate Rule 4 keeps it there; `src/devforge/lib/_shared/memory.py` is the single owner of the path literal, the three-state probe (`absent`/`stub`/`populated`), and the bounded excerpt/digest reads, which are byte-identical to what callers did before so the sweep landed without touching their suites. `/review` + `/audit` were a BEHAVIOR FIX, not a rename — they now read real memory for the first time. (2) The `/specify` gate can now FAIL: `record-input-read` PROBES the file itself (deliberately no CLI flag supplies the state — a caller-supplied value would rebuild the same unfalsifiable gate), `phase1-finalize` rejects a record with no probe-produced state, and `absent`/`stub` both PASS because a memory-less fresh install is CORRECT. (3) The seven orphaned preflight payloads now have named consuming surfaces, enforced by `scripts/verify-memory-lane.py`. (4) `/implement`'s agent brief is DIRECTED, not permissive. **The gate's Rule 2 is CONJUNCTIVE — do not loosen it to a disjunction**; a mandatory regression test pins that a `READS` command whose only touchpoint is a preflight FAILS. It was built BEFORE the wire-in, failed with 13 violations, and that list was the worklist. **Honest scope, stated by the checker itself:** it verifies a read happens AND that a surface names the field; it does NOT verify the consumption is SUBSTANTIVE, that the right entries were selected, or that memory content is accurate — and Rule 3 proves only that no KNOWN token appears. **A green gate is not a claim about memory quality.** Note `/implement`'s D7 edit is NOT forced by the gate (its `main.md` already contains `memory_digest` in a field list) — its only falsifiable check is `grep -n "may read the full file" src/commands/implement/references/agent-brief.md` returning zero. **OQ-5 closed a live data-loss bug:** `install.sh` was erasing accumulated memory on re-install; the fix is preserve-and-restore by COPY (not move) with a pre-existing-backup check, after review reproduced TWO crash windows in which a move-aside version destroyed the data it protected — one of them while printing "preserved (not overwritten)". **Four follow-ups were deliberately NOT taken at the time, each a separate decision — TWO have since been CLOSED, TWO remain OPEN.** **✅ CLOSED by `f2b0366` (2026-08-13, shipped in 2.0.9):** `.devforge/project-config.json` had the IDENTICAL install-clobber exposure with a larger blast radius (it ships as an all-null stub, `/devforge:configure` populates it, `install.sh` had zero references to it) — `install.sh` now preserves it alongside `memory.md`, the two guards refactored into one `_PRESERVE` loop so a third file is a one-word change; and running the test suite wrote `src/devforge/.generate-docs-trace.log`, which made `install.sh` abort on its own stray-file guard — the offending test now points `DEVFORGE_DIR` at a throwaway dir instead of unsetting it (the premise was wrong, not the guard), with the same default set suite-wide in `tests/conftest.py` as defence in depth. **⏳ STILL OPEN, unchanged:** six of the seven preflights compute `memory_state` and DROP it, so their specs judge stub-vs-populated by inspecting an excerpt; and `src/agents/architect.md` Rule 8 has the architect read memory independently, so it now arrives by two routes. Original subject: `.devforge/memory.md` (the framework's persistent cross-session lessons file) is **write-mostly**, and the failure has FOUR distinct mechanisms a pure path-sweep leaves three of alive. (1) **Dead path** — `/specify`, `/audit`, `/plan`, `/breakdown` cite a nonexistent memory location in prose, as do three helper preflights and one manifest entry; nothing errors, the read just returns nothing forever. (2) **A forcing function that certifies a read of a nonexistent file** — `/specify`'s mandatory four-base-read gate includes that dead path and its recording verb validates only that a *string* was passed, so every `/specify` run in history has recorded reading a file that never existed and the gate went green on it. (3) **Orphaned payload** — seven preflights build a memory excerpt field that **zero** command specs consume (plan 41's type-3 orphan, applied to data instead of findings). (4) **Truncation** — the one command with a consumed read takes a 5-line digest, and for any memory file whose additions came only from the pipeline's own writers that digest is provably the shipped stub preamble and nothing else. Proposed shape: one shared path resolver under `_shared/` (the literal is currently duplicated across ~13 helper files and ~9 command specs, and **two hand-written "do not change this path" comments already exist** — direct evidence that convention failed to hold the invariant); a dead-path sweep; a three-state probe `absent` / `stub` / `populated` (a bare existence check would be near-vacuous, since the installer ships the stub into every install); an intake wire-in for `/research` + `/discover`, carrying entries as **candidate evidence sources and constraints**, never as findings; and a per-command disposition table (every emitted command is `READS` or `N/A` **with a recorded reason**, proposed 13/7) enforced by a maintainer-side checker on plan 41's model. **⚠ ONE ITEM WITH LIVE DATA AT RISK, STILL UNFIXED (OQ-5):** `install.sh`'s bulk `cp -R` of the shipped `.devforge/` template copies the 13-line memory **stub over an existing populated `.devforge/memory.md`** — the stray-user-state guard beside it does not list `memory.md`, deliberately, because memory genuinely IS a framework-shipped template at first install. So **re-running `install.sh` against a populated consumer install erases accumulated memory.** Recommended fix is a one-line copy-only-when-absent guard; the alternative manifest route is explicitly NOT recommended and its viability was NOT verified. This is unrelated to the read lane and does not wait on ratification of anything else. **Explicit Non-goals:** no new always-on `CLAUDE.md` rule (the obligation already exists in two independent always-on channels and neither prevented the incident — the gap is enumerated-phase execution, not awareness); no change to what memory contains or how it is curated; no touch to plan 73.

- `75-INVESTIGATION-SEARCH-HARNESS-PLAN.md` — **✅ DONE (build) 2026-08-14** on `develop-2.0-init` — Phase 0 RATIFIED (items (a)–(k) recorded), Phase 1 BUILT (working tree; 17 files, +2642/−26), **Phase 2 CUT, Phase 3 DEFERRED** (both retained and marked), Phase 4 docs reconcile DONE. **Phase 5 (consumer e2e) is DEFERRED by the maintainer, who intends to run it later — it is NOT waived and NOT executed.** That is plan 74's disposition, deliberately NOT plan 73's WAIVED one; read it precisely — nothing here is optional or closed. The consequence: the mechanism is **build-verified, NOT consumer-validated** — nothing in Phase 1 has been exercised against a real consumer install, so **a future session must not read "DONE" as evidence the harness works.** Per the plan's own D1 bound a step can be forced to run and its output forced to be recorded but cannot be verified to have noticed the right thing, and **Phase 5 is the only thing that tests that**; it is a **known-answer regression anchor**, since the correct output is already known from the winning run of the originating A/B. **A closure closes phases, not items — FIVE were recorded and FOUR stay OPEN, inherited by any future session.** (1) — **✅ CLOSED by `1342ab4` (2026-08-15, shipped in 2.0.9).** The truncation test against Phase 1's added prose, recorded here as NOT RUN, has now been run. It found three sentences whose opening clause licensed less work than the full sentence required, all reordered so the duty precedes the permission: step 4's opening (which truncated before its path set, licensing a sweep of the literal's own file alone — the very blind spot the passage names), step 4's closer (which led with "gates nothing" before the sentence justifying the work), and plan 73's `deliberate` evidence arm (which opened with the permission before the citation duty — the exact clause whose truncation produced the motivating incident, and which had survived unedited behind a correctly-added branch because every prior review checked whether the new qualification was sound rather than the ordering inside the arm it routed to). **It surfaced a NEW open item in closing this one:** the fix-layer twin of that `deliberate` arm carries the same latent defect and is deliberately untouched — it was verified byte-unchanged as a recorded criterion, so it needs its own pass. Then **four deferred follow-ups, NONE fixed:** (2) `_discover/_cmds_absence.py`'s `deleted_commit_sha` carries the same permissive-setter SHA shape as the three sites Phase 1 normalized — harmless **today only** because `absence_probes` is not modeled in a typed schema, so no strict check downstream can collide with it, and a real bug the moment that changes; (3) **check 9 accepts a `declared_total` of `0` that its own setter (`cmd_declare_caller_total`) hard-rejects** — a verify-layer floor that does not mirror the setter's, reachable only by direct state mutation today; (4) there is **no shared SHA-normalizing accessor**, so the three sites were fixed independently and a fourth field will repeat the pattern; (5) there is **no cross-render field-coverage check**, which is what let `introduced_when` be dropped by one render while another showed it, for the life of the feature. The item-by-item disposition lives in the plan's `### Phase 0 ratification record (2026-08-14)`; read it before reopening any cut or deferral. Commit state is SPLIT, as with 73 and 74: the code half is in the working tree, while the plan DOCUMENT stays deliberately untracked because it carries private-client identifiers and this repo is PUBLIC. Created 2026-08-12 from the same two-install A/B that seeded 73 and 74, read for a third, different failure. Thesis (the maintainer's, and it governs the plan): gathering the right data and answering "why" is 75–80% of the work, so the framework's job is to be **the senior developer in search and analysis, with human verification in the loop rather than designed out of it.** Its reframe: **the losing run cleared every gate the framework had** — mandatory runner-up framing, a CONFIRMED falsifier, a REFUTED hypothesis, complete and correctly classified caller enumeration — and reached the wrong conclusion anyway. **It lost on curiosity, not on rigor**, so more gates of the kind plan 73 built would not have prevented it. Four missing search steps: (1) read the persistent memory file at INTAKE — **owned by plan 74, not built here**; this plan contributes only the constraint that the read must be at intake, because a memory entry can name an evidence SOURCE (changing what gets searched) and not merely a hazard (changing what gets flagged); (2) **widen archaeology past the introducing commit** — the sharpest fact in the plan is that **plan 73's evidence arm can fire, run correctly, and still reach the wrong conclusion** because the commit that made the value a leftover never touched the literal at all; **BUILT as Phase 1 2026-08-14** (17 files, +2642/−26): step 1's `Multi-commit history is OUT OF SCOPE` clause is REPLACED so the introducing commit now marks where the window STARTS rather than where it closes, and a new step 4 sweeps `git log <introducing-commit-sha>..HEAD` over the literal's file AND its Phase 2.4c inbound callers, the result carried on the EXISTING `record-literal-archaeology` call via a new OPTIONAL `--supply-changing-commits` flag with **three states — absent = not swept · `[]` = swept, found nothing · populated = the commits** ("nobody looked" and "looked, clean" are different facts), rendered for a human in a new `### Supply-changing commits since introduction` section and carried to `/devforge:plan`'s architect brief; (3) resolve a load-bearing literal to its definition, recording "no definition found" as a **positive finding** — **CUT as Phase 2 on BLAST RADIUS AND ANCHORING, NOT on evidence count** (record that distinction, or a future session revives it on the wrong trigger): Phase 1 is bounded by machinery that already exists — plan 73's archaeology trigger pre-selects the literals, the window has a defined start, and the path set is plans 67/69's enumerated inbound callers — whereas Phase 2 fired on "each load-bearing literal" with NO selector, and D2 forbids a config flag to turn it down; plan 74 also absorbed the load-bearing half of its rationale, since `research/main.md:51` now names "a related repository that is readable locally" at intake. **Revival trigger: a scoped trigger, or a second observed incident — not merely a second incident.** (4) read the AREA's history, not the line's — mechanically anchored on the already-enumerated inbound callers from plans 67/69, and folded into Phase 1's single sweep rather than given a phase of its own, that sweep's path set BEING those enumerated callers. **D1 is the plan's spine and its main risk: search steps, NOT gates** — a step can be forced to run and its output forced to be recorded, but **it cannot be verified to have noticed the right thing**. The plan names its own **standing hazard**: gates are cheap to prove, search steps are not, so a build session under pressure for a green artifact will want to convert a step into "check 21". Its tripwire is a per-phase Verify criterion — no new check number in the diff — **plus a second criterion for UNNUMBERED validators**, because plan 73 shipped two hard-fail gates carrying no check number at all, so a `Check N` grep would pass an imitation of them in letter while violating the tripwire in substance. **Depends on two of plan 73's phases and carries a fallback arm for each** (both retained-as-record, per this repo's convention of keeping arms not taken): its **D6** extends 73's evidence-lane declaration with new lane vocabulary — depend arm LIVE since that phase shipped, fallback arm (build its own declaration) NOT TAKEN and kept, since the reasoning revives if the mechanism is removed; D6 rides **Phase 3, which was DEFERRED** on sequencing (lane vocabulary describes WHICH STEPS RAN, so it cannot precede the step set) and on cost (8 production files / 11 edit sites / 43 test invocations for one boolean). Its **D5** carrier collision LANDED on the extend arm — a new optional flag on the archaeology row 73's `/plan` carry owns — not on the sibling-field alternative. **The Phase 1 build also fixed TWO PRE-EXISTING bugs** (neither introduced by it): `_dict_to_dataclass` could not reconstruct `Optional[List[<dataclass>]]`, so a populated sweep made `import-handoff` raise — a run failing *because* the sweep found something — and the `/plan`-side render had NEVER emitted `introduced_when`, a required schema field, while `_research/_render.py` showed it in a column. **⚠ HONEST BOUND — do not read "Phase 1 BUILT" as "the harness works":** built means the sweep RUNS and its three states survive end to end; per D1 it does NOT mean the step noticed the right thing. **Do not go hunting for stale text:** the `STILL UNBUILT` strings that still grep in `75-…-PLAN.md` sit INSIDE its own 2026-08-12 correction records, not in live claims — do not re-correct them (`plan_helper.py` carries the `**Literal provenance**` block, verified 2026-08-12). Other open items: **OQ-1**, the plan's central unresolved tension (what makes a search step run if gates are the wrong instrument — the honest resolution is a **division**, not a synthesis: presence of output may be recorded and checked, the CONCLUSION is never adjudicated); **OQ-2**, the "redo the ticket" case — verified **structurally inexpressible** today, since `/research`'s verdict enum has no such value and the nearest one terminates the run instead of routing anywhere, deliberately left to a sibling plan; **OQ-3 is NO LONGER OPEN** — Phase 1's build landed the widened sweep INSIDE the existing archaeology phase as a new step 4, not in a phase of its own. **D2 — discovered, not configured:** no per-project investigation-depth flag, because the setup command runs before anyone has investigated anything and history quality varies WITHIN a codebase, not just between codebases.
