- `77-POST-CHANGE-OUTPUT-MATRIX-PLAN.md` — **DONE (build) 2026-08-15 — Phase 0 RATIFIED, Phase 2 SHIPPED into `src/`, Phases 1 and 3 WAIVED (not deferred). Every status claim in the body below predates that and is superseded by the STATUS AMENDMENT that closes this entry — read it first.** Created 2026-08-12 on `develop-2.0-init`. The plan was drafted with no code, no `src/` edit, no `main.md` edit and no `src/agents/` edit behind it, and every decision carries its own counter-argument **(the draft-time "and none is settled" is no longer true — Phase 0 ratified them; see the closing amendment for which)**. Subject: a proposed **post-change output matrix** at `/plan` — for any decision that REMOVES OR SUPPRESSES an emitted value, the architect must produce, BEFORE the decision is recorded, a one-row-per-call-site table (`Call site (file:line) | Inputs it passes | Emits after the change | Verdict | Note`) whose "emits after" cell is TRACED rather than asserted and whose verdict is `live`/`dead`, after which the decision table's `Why` column CITES rows instead of restating them. **Its governing criterion is the plan's most reusable output and is meant to be quoted:** *does the rule produce an artifact that is visibly wrong when the analysis wasn't done?* Two candidate rules are recorded as FAILING it so they are not re-proposed — "enumerate the consumers" (a list of consumers that must NOT change satisfies it) and a completeness count (a matrix with every row marked `live` passes the count and hides the defect perfectly). The matrix's filled rows clear the bar on CONTENT grounds: a row saying an unintended caller still emits the value being removed reads wrong on its face, with no design principle needed. **Rule 4 of its five is already built in v2 and is the only one that is** — the dead-code declaration → typed carry → `/verify` absence check plan 71 shipped (`/plan` sub-question 9 → the conditional `### Change-Induced Dead Code` table → `DeadCodeRow` → `/breakdown`'s `verify-dead-code-coverage` → `_verify/_dead_code.py::check_dead_code_removal`); the matrix's `dead` rows FEED that chain unchanged, and the plan's non-goals forbid touching any part of it. **Rule 1's honest bound, recorded as an accepted residual rather than a solved problem:** enumeration completeness is bounded by the mechanical trace it rests on and is NOT independently checkable by the reader — a silently missing row (graph incompleteness, dynamic dispatch, reflection) is indistinguishable from a complete matrix at the approval gate — so rule 1 is the floor the content-checkable rules stand on, not a self-verifying artifact. **The measurement design is the spine, not an appendix:** every benchmark number behind the plan is v1.28 from `main` (that arm scored 2/2 on discovering the hidden coupling and 0/2 on handling it; nine runs across three competing harnesses scored 0/9 on both), and **v2 has never been run against that ticket**, so Phase 1 is a blind v2 BASELINE run that PRECEDES any `src/` edit (D5 — a deliberate inversion of this repo's build-then-e2e convention) and two of its four outcomes close the plan with no edit at all: a baseline that already handles the hidden surface deletes the mechanism before it is built, and a baseline that neither discovers nor acts routes a discovery regression to a sibling plan instead. D6 makes the delete branch first-class at the other end too — if Phase 3's probe does not flip, Phase 2's markdown edits are reverted in the same session as the reading. **Relationship to the FALSE BINARY finding above:** OQ-4 recommends NO constitution change, on the grounds that the matrix makes the enumeration-independent form self-evident without naming it, so a passing Phase 3 would CLOSE that finding by MOOTING it and never by answering it — and if the delete branch fires the finding is untouched and still open. **(NEITHER branch can fire — Phase 3 was WAIVED 2026-08-15. This plan does NOT moot that finding; it is untouched and still open. See the closing amendment.)** Numbered 77 rather than 76 because the `76` identity is occupied by the file-less finding entry above, whose own text tells sessions no `76-*-PLAN.md` exists. **AMENDED 2026-08-13 — read this amendment BEFORE the decisions above; R1 changes the plan's PLACEMENT, so every sentence above that sites the matrix at `/plan` describes the pre-amendment frame.** The maintainer ratified three amendments the same day on the evidence of a v1 benchmark handoff — 16 runs across 6 harnesses on one frozen brownfield ticket (remove some emitted values from one surface; a second, unnamed surface reaches the same shared builder). Its headline is the inversion the plan is named for: DevForge v1 named the hidden surface's file **6/6** against competitors' **1/10**, and then **5 of those 6 runs** produced escalating LOCK artifacts — an "explicitly NOT modified" list carrying that very file, a hard constraint forbidding the only discriminator that covered both surfaces, an AC asserting the removed values are still present on the hidden path, and a review action item to pin that AC with a test — i.e. v1 converts its discovery advantage into a DEFENDED defect, which is precisely why the plan's probe measures HANDLING and not discovery. **The complementary 1-of-6-passing figure is CONTESTED BY THE SOURCE ITSELF and is recorded here, not resolved:** the handoff's own summary table scores v1 `fixed: 0/6` while its mechanism section describes one of the six as passing; the maintainer was asked and did not resolve it, so this index reproduces the plan document's own caveat rather than harmonizing it. Read the per-run counts as APPROXIMATE — the MECHANISM finding (a discovery advantage converted into a defended defect) is the load-bearing content and does not move with the count. The same contested figure underwrites the "sole passing run" arithmetic in the FALSE BINARY finding above, which is therefore approximate in the same way and for the same reason. **These figures do not replace the v1.28 numbers cited earlier in this entry — they enlarge the sample (2 v1 runs → 6; 9 competitor runs → 10, totalling the 16) and are stated in terms of NAMING the hidden surface's file, which is not word-for-word the earlier "discovering the hidden coupling"; a session reconciling the two goes to the handoff, not to this index.** **R1 — the matrix moves from `/plan` to `/research`**: it is produced BEFORE ACs exist at `/specify` (so a lock cannot be written into an AC before the matrix exists to contradict it), its row source is the plan-67/69 caller-enumeration machinery already native to `/research`, and its carrier is a required section of `research-report.md` read in place downstream on the plan-53 park-once precedent — preserving D4's instruction-only, cheaply-deletable shape **(that carrier clause was FALSIFIED and REPLACED 2026-08-15 — read the 2026-08-15 CARRIER AMENDMENT below, the one introducing A1, before acting on it; R1's stage and row source stand, its carrier does not)**; `/plan` becomes the matrix's CONSUMER and verifier, and plan 71's sub-question-9 dead-code chain is fed unchanged. **R2 — stricter Phase 1/3 acceptance**: the handling probe passes only if the hidden surface stops emitting the removed values WITHOUT its file appearing in the diff, plus a secondary artifact-signature grep for assertions that a removed value is still present. **R3 — a sixth D1 rule** (derived exclusions: an out-of-scope / "not modified" entry is valid only when its matrix row shows an EMPTY intersection between what that caller still emits and what the ticket removes; non-empty ⇒ escalate as a product question) **plus an instruction-only `/specify` directive** — an AC asserting the continued presence of a removed value requires quoted product intent. A review-side matrix-reading question (the one question that can invalidate an AC) is recorded as a CONDITIONAL follow-on, explicitly NOT Phase-2 scope. **Phase 0 STILL has not run** — D1–D6 and OQ-1–OQ-6 as amended are all still unratified, and OQ-1's `/plan`-sub-question placement analysis is superseded IN FRAME by R1 (its reasoning is retained, its stage is not); **as of 2026-08-15 A1's sibling-file carrier joins R1–R3 as a ratified input to that gate, and it re-resolves OQ-3's recorded carrier resolution the same way R1 superseded OQ-1's placement analysis — the decisions themselves stay unratified, and Phase 0 still has not run.** **(SUPERSEDED 2026-08-15 — Phase 0 RAN and was ratified later the same day; see the closing amendment.)** The amendment text lands in the plan itself as `R1`–`R3` by a parallel edit that was in flight when this entry was written — *if `grep -n "R1" 77-POST-CHANGE-OUTPUT-MATRIX-PLAN.md` returns nothing, that plan-side record never landed and this index entry is the only copy.* **The evidence behind all three is `77-EVIDENCE-DISCOVERY-TO-LOCK-INVERSION.md` at repo root — UNTRACKED and to stay that way**: it is the verbatim private-client handoff, it carries client identifiers, and it must never be committed or quoted into any tracked file (same disposition as the untracked plans 73/74/75 — plan 78's delete-at-v2-go-live route, not a scrub). It shares plan 77's `77-` filename prefix but is EVIDENCE, not a second plan file; a `77-*` glob returns both. Null results from the same dataset, recorded here because they close off the cheap fixes first: artifact volume (24×) and test volume (14×) had ZERO effect on the probe, investigation depth INVERTED the relationship, 15 of the 16 runs added a caller-side opt-out parameter, and guard shape alone predicted the outcome **17/17** (17 guard-shape data points over 16 runs — the counts differ; the handoff is the authority, this index is not). **CARRIER AMENDMENT, 2026-08-15 (the first of two same-dated amendments; the STATUS AMENDMENT closing this entry is later and supersedes its status claims) — read this before R1 above: R1's CARRIER was falsified and replaced; its stage, its row source and its `/plan`-as-consumer half stand unchanged.** A pre-implementation verification pass checked R1's "required section of `research-report.md`" against that command's live render contract and falsified it: `src/commands/research/main.md:1114` locks report composition inside the helper ("The orchestrator does NOT compose this markdown; the helper owns the section order … heading levels, and table shapes") and `:1120` forbids the LLM from touching the rendered bytes at all ("Phase 4 writes the helper's rendered bytes verbatim and never reshapes them") — so a required section of that report would have cost a schema field, a setter verb and a renderer change, the exact accumulation D4 and the plan's own tripwire forbid; the matrix's own row source is not in that report today either (`src/devforge/lib/_research/_render.py` holds a single composer, `_render_report_md`, naming neither enumeration field). **A1, ratified by the maintainer 2026-08-15, replaces the carrier with a SIBLING file** — `specs/<dirname>/emission-matrix.md`, orchestrator-written during `/devforge:research` Phase 4 beside the byte-verbatim report and read in place downstream. R1's substance survives intact: production at `/devforge:research` before any acceptance criterion exists, a document artifact rather than a handoff schema field, park-once/read-in-place on the plan-53 precedent. **D4's instruction-only, cheaply-deletable property is preserved by the REPLACEMENT, not by R1 as originally written** — R1 ASSERTED that property while mandating a section of a helper-owned report that could not carry one without Python; the sibling file makes it literally true. Do not compress this into "R1 preserved D4": the distinction is the entire content of the correction. **A3 changes HOW plan 71's sub-question-9 dead-code chain is fed, never WHETHER** — the matrix now produces no `dead` rows at all, because reachability is a consequence of a guard that does not exist until `/devforge:plan` chooses one; the architect still derives every `### Change-Induced Dead Code` row from sub-question 9's own fresh trace, taking the matrix's `affected` rows as the input set it must account for. The shipped chain is untouched and the plan's non-goals still forbid touching it — the join is enumeration-to-obligation, not row-to-row — so the earlier sentences above describing a `live`/`dead` verdict and `dead` rows that FEED that chain describe the pre-A3 frame; go to the plan for the current row shape. **As of this CARRIER AMENDMENT the plan was still unstarted with no `src/` file edited for it** — A1 was a carrier decision recorded in the plan document, not a build, and D5's blind-baseline-before-any-`src/`-edit inversion was then untouched. **Both facts EXPIRED hours later: the plan was ratified, Phase 2 shipped into `src/`, and D5 was OVERRIDDEN by the waiver of Phase 1 — see the closing amendment. Nothing in this sentence is a live claim.** The authority is that plan's `## Amendment 2026-08-15` section: `A1` is the ratified carrier replacement, and every other `A`-numbered item — `A3`'s dead-code join included — is a Phase-0 item carrying no ratification of its own. **(Phase 0 has since RUN — the closing amendment names exactly which items were ratified. The shipped build realizes A3's enumeration-to-obligation join, which is a statement of what shipped and NOT a claim that the `A`-numbered items were individually ratified.)** **STATUS AMENDMENT, 2026-08-15 (later the same day than the CARRIER AMENDMENT above) — this supersedes every status claim in this entry, its opening marker included: Phase 0 RATIFIED, Phase 2 SHIPPED into `src/`, Phases 1 and 3 WAIVED.** The maintainer confirmed the plan's own recommendation on every open item — D1's artifact shape, D2 option (b), D3, D4's instruction-only scope, OQ-1's fact-keyed `/devforge:research` trigger, OQ-2's re-keyed columns and equivalence-class row collapse, OQ-3 as re-resolved by A1's sibling-file carrier earlier the same day, OQ-4's no-constitution-change, and OQ-5's carry of plan 69's wrong-symbol cross-check by citation; OQ-6 is moot. **Phases 1 and 3 are WAIVED, NOT deferred — this list reserves "deferred" for a check the maintainer still intends to run, and the distinction is load-bearing here.** No blind v2 baseline was run and no handling probe will ever be read, so **D5's measure-before-build inversion is OVERRIDDEN and D6 retires with it** — a delete branch that fires on a probe result cannot survive the probe's cancellation — and every sentence above describing the measurement design, its baseline outcomes, R2's probe acceptance, or the revert-on-no-flip branch describes a design that will not be executed. **State the cost plainly and do not soften it:** this is the plan whose own evidence was that artifact volume does not move outcomes, and whose falsification test was a blind before/after run on a frozen prompt; that test will not happen, so the shipped mechanism now rests entirely on the argument that a wrongly-filled row reads wrong on its face — **reasoning, not evidence.** A future session must NOT read shipped code here as a validated result. **Consequence for the FALSE BINARY finding above: this plan does NOT moot it.** The mooting was conditional on a passing Phase 3, that probe is waived, and OQ-4's ratification decides only that THIS plan makes no constitution change — so that finding is **untouched, still open, and now without a designated resolution path**; the conditional-mooting sentence earlier in this entry describes a route that no longer exists. **Phase 2 shipped instruction-only**, editing `src/commands/research/main.md` (PRODUCES the artifact — a Phase-3 compose step, a conditional Phase-4 write step, and the commit path), `src/commands/plan/main.md` (CONSUMES and VERIFIES it — the architect brief file list, a widened sub-question 7 output, a new sub-question, a prohibition against lifting an anchor token from a carried cell, a PHASE 2.5 backstop step, an approval-summary line, and rule 5 labelled), `src/commands/specify/main.md` (a conditional read plus an acceptance-criteria directive requiring quoted product intent — R3's instruction-side half, now shipped), `src/agents/architect.md` (new Rule 9 forcing steps) and `src/CLAUDE.md` (an Artifact Storage tree line). **Zero Python, zero schema fields, zero helper verbs, zero exit codes — D4 honoured.** The artifact is `specs/<dirname>/emission-matrix.md`, one row per call site, verdict **`affected` / `unaffected` only, carrying NO reachability claim** — so plan 71's dead-code chain is fed unchanged and every anchor token still comes from the architect's own fresh trace; the `live`/`dead` verdict and the column list quoted earlier in this entry are the pre-ratification shape, so go to the plan for the current one. **Verification is BUILD verification ONLY** — an instruction-reviewer pass over the whole diff raised findings and every one was fixed, and a claude-code-guide pass returned conformant, but nothing has been run in a consumer install and the artifact has never been produced by a real run. **Known residuals, none fixed:** the artifact auto-tags `source_origin = "prior_spec"` at `/devforge:specify` because the helper's filename dispatch knows only the two intake reports, and fixing that needs helper code the instruction-only scope excludes; Rule 9's `Why` cell has no ordering convention now that a third annotation can land in it; no durable carrier distinguishes "no matrix was applicable" from "the step was skipped"; and the feature is unmeasured. **AMENDED 2026-08-18 (plan 81 D3, ratified): disposition = KEEP AND AMEND, and plan 81 is the amendment.** An external 20-run, 6-framework benchmark effectively ran this plan’s Phase-3 ARM (a matrix-present run, no Phase-1 blind baseline): the matrix is vindicated ON DISCOVERY — it named the coupled call site 16 of 20 runs never named — while the handling probe FAILED through a chain of wrong INFERENCES from correctly-computed facts (plan 81’s subject). **Bounded reading is mandatory: an "after" with no "before" — consistent with the matrix working, NOT a demonstration that it did** (this plan’s own OQ-6 bound; one run separates nothing). Under the retired D6 a non-flipping probe was the delete branch; the evidence supports keep-and-amend because discovery value is now measured and the failure localizes to named inference steps, which plan 81’s six rules address. Nothing in plan 77’s file is edited and no sentence of this entry is withdrawn.

- `78-TEST-IDENTIFIER-SCRUB-PLAN.md` — **NOT STARTED — awaiting Phase-0 ratification; D1–D8 and OQ-1–OQ-6 are all unanswered.** Created 2026-08-12 on `develop-2.0-init`. Two carve-outs from that status, both recorded inside the plan: the deletion disposition below is marked RATIFIED, and one working-tree substitution pass is already applied (full suite green) with a second in flight. Subject: scrubbing private-client identifiers out of this PUBLIC repo's test fixtures, plus a **mechanical recurrence guard** so the class cannot silently come back. Origin: a 2026-08-12 review found client identifiers in tracked test fixtures, and **the first scrub pass was verified by a grep that returned zero hits and was still incomplete** — it grepped the four tokens it had just substituted, so it structurally could not find two commit-SHA-shaped strings that were never in the substitution table, and those survivors functioned as a DE-ANONYMIZATION KEY (other files in the repo pair them with the identifiers that had been replaced). That failure mode, not the identifiers, shapes every phase: D2 makes inventory-BEFORE-substitution mandatory, because a substitution performed before the inventory exists can only ever be verified against itself. **The central design move is D3, an inversion:** the inventory of REAL identifiers is unpublishable by definition and lives OUTSIDE the repository (referenced by an execution-time path — it cannot be committed, and gitignored-but-present is not a home either, since an ignore-rule edit or a `git add -f` re-exposes it), while the **allowlist of approved SYNTHETIC values ships IN-repo precisely because it contains no client data** — so the guard asks "is every shape-bearing literal a declared synthetic?", which is answerable entirely from public data, instead of "does any literal match a real identifier?", which would require the secret in-repo. **Its coverage bound is stated honestly and is to be carried into the code, not just the plan:** the guard structurally MISSES every NAME class — company, product, repository, component, prop, symbol, package prefix — because none has a distinguishing shape, and those are exactly the classes the first pass found BY HAND; what it catches is the shape-bearing classes (commit SHAs, ticket-ID-shaped tokens, absolute paths), which is precisely what SURVIVED that pass. In the plan's own sentence: a shape-based check catches CLASSES, a list-based check catches INSTANCES, and they are not substitutes for each other. **Scope is `tests/` ONLY (D1)** — three test files (two tracked, one untracked); `src/`, `scripts/` and `docs/` measured at zero hits, so nothing the framework ships to consumers is exposed or touched. **Plan documents and `done-plans/` are EXCLUDED, not deferred:** the maintainer RATIFIED deleting them at the official v2 go-live rather than scrubbing them, and the plan states the limitation plainly — on a public repository deletion does not remove anything from git history, and a history rewrite is an explicit non-goal routed to a separate decision. Guard shape per D5 mirrors plan 41 exactly (`scripts/lib/` scanner + `scripts/` CLI + a `tests/lib/` test against the live tree, which is the only mechanism that runs automatically here — the repo has no CI config, no Makefile and no pytest config file), with D4 loud-SKIP-never-silent-pass when the inventory is unavailable, D6 hard-fail on undeclared shape-bearing literals but ADVISORY-only on name-shaped ones (**a future session must not strengthen that advisory arm into a gate** without first solving the false-positive cost Phase 4 measures), and D7 synthetic replacements that preserve assertion-relevant SHAPE (character class, length, token ordering, repetition) while discarding meaning. The opt-in consumer-side pre-commit forcing-functions hook was REJECTED as the guard's host on two verified grounds (it exits 0 silently when `.devforge/constitute.json` or the installed helper is absent — both absent in this repo — and putting a maintainer privacy rule in a consumer-shipped surface would export this repo's problem to every consumer). OQ-5 recommends scanning file PATHS as well as contents, since a content scan cannot see a filename by construction; its demonstrating case is a repo-root plan-tier document whose FILENAME embeds a ticket-ID-shaped token — the same property the plan-67 entry above now records for the document it cites. OQ-1 (whether the maintainer's own test-harness project name counts as an identifier to scrub — the only path in the plan that reaches `src/`; recommendation EXCLUDE) is explicitly the maintainer's call, and the plan deliberately declines to write that name anywhere so it stays correct under either answer.

- `79-MEMORY-WINDOW-AND-RECEIPT-PLAN.md` — **✅ DONE (build) 2026-08-18 on `develop-2.0-init`; Phase 6 consumer e2e DEFERRED — maintainer intends to run it (NOT waived; build-verified, NOT consumer-validated).** Created 2026-08-17. The plan document carries no private-client identifiers and is **committed normally**, unlike the deliberately-untracked plans 73/74/75. **The defect: `.devforge/memory.md` had two writers and twelve readers, and every writer wrote to the BOTTOM while every reader read the TOP.** The installer ships a 13-line stub (`## Architecture Decisions` / `## Known Pitfalls` / `## What Worked` / `## What Failed`, and **no** `## Task Outcomes`); `_append_under_section` creates a missing section at EOF, so `/devforge:implement`'s one-receipt-per-approved-task writer created `## Task Outcomes` at the bottom and it stayed last forever; `/devforge:verify` PHASE 7 appended its feature-level lesson at EOF naming no section, landing it inside that same last section; and the bounded read returned `lines[:40]`. **Past roughly 25 accumulated lines the readable window froze on the stub preamble plus the oldest receipts — the receipt writer evicted the lesson writer**, and `/devforge:verify`'s lessons are the only lesson-shaped content the pipeline produces. **The clincher: `src/commands/implement/references/agent-brief.md:12` already documented the identical arithmetic for the 5-line digest and routed `/devforge:implement`'s agent brief around it with a direct file read — the same reasoning at n=40 was never generalized, and it takes ~25 more lines to bite, which is why it survived.** **Move (4) below removed that documented sentence, and the digest with it — do not go looking for either; the direct file read STAYS, for its selection rationale.** **Plan 74 did not miss it**: that gate verifies a read HAPPENS and that a surface NAMES the field, never that the consumption is substantive or the content useful, and it declares that non-coverage itself — this is the content half of the same defect and reopens no plan-74 decision. **Four build moves.** **(1) Phase 1 (`c5766a5`) — the excerpt became section-aware** in `src/devforge/lib/_shared/memory.py`: `## ` sections parsed (`### ` is section CONTENT, not a boundary), the installer-shipped title dropped, `## Task Outcomes` dropped by a NAMED DENYLIST with unknown sections INCLUDED by default (D3 Option A — a denylist's failure mode is visible noise, an allowlist's is the silent loss this plan exists to fix), content-free sections dropped heading and all (reusing the existing `_is_populated_line`, never a second content predicate), an equal per-section share of a 120-line budget with unused share redistributed once in file order (OQ-1 Option B), the NEWEST entries surviving a cut (D5 — an entry is added at the end of its section, so newest is last), and every cut DECLARED by a marker line under that section's heading (D4 Option A — inside the `excerpt` string, the only placement reaching all eleven consumers with zero call-site changes). **The `present` / `memory_state` / `excerpt` return contract is unchanged, which is what made this a one-function fix** — the payoff of plan 74's consolidation. A `stub` now returns `""` (four empty headings are not content), and a receipts-only file returns `""` while still probing `populated`: **the probe is UNTOUCHED (D6)** and that divergence is intended — the probe answers *does this file carry content*, the read answers *does it carry content a consumer should act on*. The probe's one production caller, `/devforge:specify`'s falsifiable plan-74 gate, is unaffected. **(2) Phase 2 (`6df0f16`) — `/devforge:implement` stopped writing per-task receipts** (D2): the write block and `_build_memory_entry` deleted; `--last-task-number` / `--last-task-title` removed together with the `src/commands/implement/main.md` arguments that passed them **in the same commit** (OQ-5 Option A — a helper dropping the flags while the spec still passed them exits non-zero on "unrecognized arguments" for every approved task, so the sequencing is a constraint, not a preference). **`/devforge:implement`'s memory READ is deliberately retained**; receipts are session-state material and `.devforge/session-state.md` already tracks the last three tasks. **(3) Phase 4.5's YES arm (2026-08-18) — `/devforge:verify` PHASE 7 now targets a NAMED bucket**: a three-line rubric selects `## What Failed` (a defect verification CAUGHT) / `## What Worked` (a technique or approach that worked) / `## Known Pitfalls` (a gotcha or near-miss), **FIRST matching line wins, no fourth bucket**, and a lesson matching none is not written (the pre-existing skip-silently path); the entry is inserted immediately before the next same-depth `## ` heading, or before EOF when the chosen section is last; **an absent heading is CREATED rather than falling back to an EOF append**; and the instruction states WHY placement matters (an EOF append lands inside whatever section happens to be last — `## Task Outcomes` on any install that ran tasks under the retired writer, the one section the excerpt drops). It reproduces `_append_under_section`'s placement rule **in prose, deliberately NOT as a helper call** — Phase 2 removed that writer, and a second memory writer or a helper verb for this write is an explicit Non-goal. **One mechanics sentence is load-bearing: the Write tool OVERWRITES, so "append with Write" does not exist** — the instruction directs Read → compose the full new content → Write it back, or the Edit tool against a unique anchor. **(4) Phase 5 (2026-08-18) — `/devforge:implement` now reads what every other consumer reads** (D7 + OQ-2): its preflight moved from `read_memory_digest` to `read_memory_context`, so the emitted JSON key is `memory_excerpt` rather than `memory_digest`, and `src/commands/implement/main.md`'s preflight-contract line was rewritten in the same change — a key rename that skipped that line would leave the orchestrator reading a field the helper no longer emits. **The single read brought TWO more fields with it**: `read_memory_context` yields present/state/excerpt from one open, so the preflight now emits `memory_present` and `memory_state` alongside the excerpt, and that contract line went from six keys to EIGHT. Both new fields are given a disposition in the spec rather than left as payload nobody reads — on `absent` or `stub` the brief's Edge-cases bullet is simply absent, which is what `references/agent-brief.md` already said for a missing file. **`read_memory_digest` is DELETED**, and its name left `scripts/lib/memory_lane.py`'s `_MEMORY_READ_CALL_RE` alternation in the same commit: that staleness is undetectable by running the gate, which exits 0 with a dead name in its regex either way. **`references/agent-brief.md`'s direct-file-read paragraph is KEPT, and that is the whole of D7's ratified scope** — the paragraph exists for SELECTION (the orchestrator holds the task contract and picks the entries bearing on this task's `touched_files`), which a section-selected read does not supply; only its now-false digest-emptiness sentence was removed, and with it the parenthetical naming an EOF append as how `/devforge:verify` files its entry — a clause move (3) had already falsified. Phase 5's own doc reconcile: `src/devforge/storage-rules.md`'s READ-lane paragraph no longer describes a separate digest read, because there is now ONE bounded read and every command is on it. **Rule 2b's green was not automatic here** — it requires `/devforge:implement`'s surfaces to keep NAMING one of `memory_excerpt` / `memory_digest` / `memory_state`, and Phase 5 edited BOTH surfaces that carried a token, so the post-edit grep is a real verification step. **`constitution_digest` is deliberately untouched**: a different field, still emitted, still the first five lines of `constitution.md` — so a bare `digest` grep under `src/commands/implement/` correctly returns that one hit, and removing it to reach zero would falsify the preflight contract. **Phase 3 (`7580508`) + Phase 4 (2026-08-18) — the stale-claim reconcile.** The 30 memory-related "first 40" occurrences spanned three classes — 16 across eleven `src/commands/` files, 13 helper docstrings, and **one argparse `help=` string in `plan_helper.py`, user-facing terminal output that no `.md`-scoped sweep reaches** — and now describe the SELECTION RULE while **deliberately not restating the budget number** (a number repeated in 30 places is the defect being fixed); PHASE 7's dead stub-headings clause, which no "first 40" grep matched, went with them. **Two "first 40" hits under `src/` are FALSE POSITIVES and must survive**: `_design_tokens/_scanner.py` (selector chars) and `_pr_review/_dispatch.py` (the 40K diff cap) — the sweep's criterion is two surviving hits, not zero. Phase 4 also reconciled `src/CLAUDE.md`'s Session-Continuity "**Not a history log**" bullet, which had designated `.devforge/memory.md` as the history home — **the sentence D2 knowingly contradicts, so a future session must not "restore" the receipt writer on its strength** — and `src/devforge/storage-rules.md`'s memory READ-lane paragraph. **Verified-and-NOT-edited, recorded so nobody re-opens them:** `src/CLAUDE.md`'s pre-flight-read line, Always rule 4 and the References link to Memory (all still true — `/devforge:implement` keeps its read), and `src/devforge/storage-rules.md`'s `implement → updates individual task file status + completion notes` lifecycle line (it makes no memory claim). **Dispositions, all ratified 2026-08-17 and recorded inline in the plan:** D3 denylist (Option A) · D4 marker inside the `excerpt` string · D5 recency within a section · D6 probe untouched · D7 migrate to `read_memory_context` and KEEP `agent-brief.md`'s selection paragraph, removing only its now-false digest-emptiness sentence · OQ-1 120-line budget + equal per-section share · OQ-2 DELETE `read_memory_digest` — DONE at Phase 5, **including removing its name from `scripts/lib/memory_lane.py`'s `_MEMORY_READ_CALL_RE` alternation in the same commit — a staleness the gate cannot detect, since it exits 0 either way** · OQ-3 YES · OQ-4 back-fill NON-GOAL · OQ-5 remove the two flags. **⚠ No back-fill, and the consequence is sharp:** on an install that already ran tasks, `## Task Outcomes` persists as the last section and the lessons already inside it stay excluded until an operator moves them out by hand. **Phase 6 step 3 therefore has two branches and picking the wrong one produces a false negative** — a lesson found INSIDE `## Task Outcomes` cannot demonstrate the fix and must be replaced as the probe by a NEW `/devforge:verify` lesson written after delivery; a failure there is the ratified OQ-4 non-goal working as designed, not a Phase-1–5 defect. **The minimum shippable unit is 1 + 2 + 3, not 1 + 3** — an earlier draft claimed the latter; without D2 the receipts keep `## Task Outcomes` last, so the exclusion merely REPLACES the eviction and gets worse, a visibly-full window being at least noticeable where a silent exclusion is not. **Build-verified, NOT consumer-validated**; Phase 6 records its result in `REGRESSION-ANCHORS.md`, naming Phase 1's case-3 test and the branch taken so a later re-run compares like with like.

- `80-CONSTITUTE-CITATION-FALSE-POSITIVE-PLAN.md` — **✅ DONE (build) 2026-08-18 on `develop-2.0-init` — closed by maintainer 2026-08-18; Phase 5 consumer e2e DEFERRED — maintainer intends to run it (NOT waived; user-driven HARD GATE; build-verified, NOT consumer-validated).** Phase 0 RATIFIED 2026-08-18 with **D1–D7 all CONFIRMED as written** and OQ-1–OQ-6 answered inline; Phases 1–3 committed `6896398` / `e74d8df` / `e823da7`; Phase 4 (spec + docs reconcile) landed this pass. Every code claim in the plan was verified against the working tree 2026-08-17 and re-verified 2026-08-18. **The defect: `/devforge:constitute` Phase 6.2's citation dimension false-positived on prose the framework's own pipeline produces.** `constitute_helper validate` is a 4-dimension weighted gate over `.devforge/constitute.json`; dimension 2 (weight 0.25) extracts path-like tokens from rule text, table cells and code-example annotations and demands each exist on disk. **The misalignment was BY CONSTRUCTION across three surfaces** — `/devforge:generate-docs` captures a conventions bucket named `naming`, `/devforge:constitute` Phase 3 routes that raw text into §3 and names "3.3 Naming Conventions" as an example sub-section, and Phase 6.2 then requires the filenames that prose necessarily contains to exist — so any component-per-file frontend layout failed a dimension whose CONTENT was correct, and the human `ship` override became the routine path, eroding the gate's signal for its real target (genuinely hallucinated file references). **The observed consumer run: `composite=0.7955 FAIL`, `citation 0.1818` (2 of 11 unique tokens resolved), the other three dimensions at 1.0** — and with those three perfect, citation must reach **≥ 0.80** for the composite to clear 0.95, arithmetic that reproduces exactly from the shipped weights. The 9 unresolved tokens fell into **five root-cause classes**: **RC1** convention placeholders (`PageXxx.vue`) that can never exist; **RC2** extension-only fragments (`.spec.ts`, `/index.md`), which match because the token regex only requires a run of path characters to END in a known extension; **RC3** a cross-slash artifact (`.ts/.vue`) — the character class spans both `.` and `/`, so an unbackticked adjacent extension pair in prose joins into one token; **RC4** real files nested below the install root, which no standalone resolution stage could ever find; **RC5** framework runtime files created LATER by the pipeline (`.devforge/session-state.md`). **D1 — fix the extractor and the resolver, never a threshold, and the argument is mechanical:** `cmd_validate`'s exit code is gated on the COMPOSITE alone, while `_DIM_PASS_THRESHOLDS` feeds only the per-dimension `"pass"` boolean in the stdout JSON, so raising the citation dimension's slop changes no pass/fail outcome at all and would be pure theatre (**OQ-1 answered NO** and recorded so a future session does not re-propose it as the cheap fix); lowering the composite bar blunts all four dimensions to fix one; deleting dimension 2 surrenders the hallucinated-citation catch that is its whole purpose. `_COMPOSITE_PASS_THRESHOLD`, `_VALIDATE_WEIGHTS` and `_DIM_PASS_THRESHOLDS` are unchanged. **Phase 1 (WI-1) — four filters, and the PLACEMENT SPLIT is load-bearing (D2/D3/D4).** Predicates: **(a)** token contains `Xxx`; **(b)** no `/` and the basename starts with `.`; **(c)** starts with `/`; **(d)** contains `/` and at least one NON-final segment is a dot-prefixed chain of known extensions. A matching token counts toward NEITHER `resolved` NOR `unresolved` and produces no `failed_items` entry. **Filter (c) runs BEFORE resolution (it is a correctness fix, see F1) and (a)/(b)/(d) run only AFTER a token fails to resolve** — so a real file whose NAME matches a filter shape (an existing `.eslintrc.json`, a real `XxxService.ts`) resolves first and still counts as a citation; applying all four at extraction time was REJECTED because it silently drops real files and weakens the gate's real target for no gain. **OQ-5 keeps predicate (b) exactly as written** — both narrower alternatives are recorded and neither adopted. Predicate (d) needs the extension vocabulary as data, so the alternation that was inline in the regex is now one named ordered constant read by both the compiled regex and the predicate, **with its ORDER preserved** (longer suffixes first, or `tsconfig.json` extracts as `tsconfig.js`) and a test pinning that. **OQ-6 ratified YES: `cmd_validate`'s stdout JSON gained `filtered_items`**, one entry per dropped token with its filter class — a silent filter is the exact failure shape this plan exists to fix, one layer up. **Its shipped shape differs from the pre-ratification wording and the difference matters if you go looking:** the collector is an OPTIONAL `filtered_out` list argument on `_count_citations` (default `None`; `cmd_validate` passes a list), NOT a widened return tuple, because the existing citation tests unpack a 4-tuple and Phase 1's Verify required them to pass unmodified. **F1, a latent bug closed by construction:** the first resolution check was `install_root_path / token`, and `pathlib` DISCARDS the left operand when the right is absolute — so `/index.md` was checked against the machine's FILESYSTEM ROOT, not the install root (the wrapper check carried the identical defect). It produced "unresolved" for the wrong reason and would have become a false RESOLVE the moment a same-named file existed at `/`. **D4 EXCLUDES rather than repairs**: stripping the slash and retrying would manufacture a resolution for a truncated-path fragment, and exclusion also guarantees no absolute token reaches that join again. **Phase 2 (WI-2, D5) — standalone got the recursive resolution wrapper mode already had.** Both `rglob` fallbacks sat inside `if effective_root is not None:`, non-`None` only for `workspace_mode == "wrapper"`, so a standalone install got an exact-path check plus a package-basename lookup **and nothing else** — RC4's direct cause, undocumented and unjustified in the code. Both stages are now root-agnostic (standalone searches install root, wrapper searches the wrapper-declared `project_root` joined onto the install root) and **both are bounded in BOTH modes** by the **OQ-2 11-name denylist** — `.git`, `node_modules`, `dist`, `build`, `target`, `vendor`, `.venv`, `venv`, `__pycache__`, `.next`, `coverage` — pruned at any depth. **OQ-4 ratified YES and the wrapper-side behavioral change is accepted, not incidental**: a token resolvable today only via a file inside `node_modules` stops resolving. **OQ-3 ratified per-token walks, no basename index** (an index cannot answer a multi-segment pattern, so it was never a drop-in). The shipped walk is `os.walk` with in-place `dirnames` pruning rather than `rglob` plus post-filtering, so it never DESCENDS into an excluded directory instead of discarding matches found inside one — and `os.walk` skips an unreadable subtree by default where `Path.rglob` propagates that `OSError`. **Named recall cost, imported from wrapper mode rather than created here:** the slash-free stage is suffix matching, so a nonexistent `Foo.vue` resolves whenever any `MyFoo.vue` exists — accepted as the price of wrapper/standalone parity. **Phase 3 (WI-3, D6) — the runtime-namespace allowance.** A token whose FIRST path segment equals the `devforge_dir` basename counts RESOLVED with no filesystem check — segment equality, not a string prefix (`.devforgex/foo.md` does not match), and the namespace name is DERIVED from the argument the counter already receives, so no path literal was introduced. **The cost is namespace-wide and is stated, not buried:** a genuinely hallucinated `.devforge/never-exists.md` resolves silently and never reaches `failed_items`, so dimension 2 stops detecting hallucinated references inside that one namespace — `test_devforge_namespace_never_exists_file_still_resolves_d6_accepted_cost` pins that outcome with the rationale in its docstring so a later session cannot read it as an oversight. The rejected allowlist alternative needs maintenance on every new runtime file, and a stale allowlist reintroduces exactly this false positive. **Phase 4 (WI-4) — spec and docs reconcile.** `src/commands/constitute/main.md` Phase 6.2 now describes the shipped mechanism in two places: the report-shape sentence gained `filtered_items`, and the dimension-2 bullet gained the recursive bounded search (with its two search roots), the four filter classes, and the runtime-namespace allowance. **Cross-ref sweep recorded per the phase's Verify: `grep -rni "citation validity" src/` returns four hits** — `src/commands/constitute/main.md` (the only prose mechanism description, updated), two in-code section comments, and `src/devforge/lib/_constitute/_cli.py`'s subcommand help string, which is a generic dimension-name mention with no mechanism description and needed no update. **What did NOT change, deliberately (the plan's Non-goals):** no producer-side rewording (**D7** — `/devforge:generate-docs`'s conventions capture and `/devforge:constitute`'s §3 routing are untouched; contorting a consumer-facing artifact to appease its own checker cannot work on freeform LLM-composed prose, and backticking does not even solve RC1/RC2/RC5); no threshold movement; the other three dimensions and the `verify` subcommand; and **the Phase 6.2 `ship` / `cancel` / `fix` human gate, which stays exactly as-is — this reduces how often it fires and a future session must not read a passing Phase 5 as licence to delete the override.** **Honest bound on filter (a), carried from D3:** `Xxx` is the observed placeholder vocabulary and nothing else, so `Foo`, `MyComponent`, `<Name>` and every other convention vocabulary still false-positives — the target is the observed classes, not the class of all convention prose. **No consumer remediation:** no migration, no backfill, no rewrite of any rendered `constitution.md`; a consumer gets the fix by re-running `install.sh` / `update.sh` (which regenerate `.devforge/lib/` from `src/devforge/lib/`) and reproduces the failing score until then. **⚠ Phase 5 is the only test that the fix works on the case that motivated it and it has NOT run.** It re-runs `validate` against the same consumer constitution that scored 0.7955 with **ZERO edits to the constitution content**, and it is a known-answer anchor: the plan carries a per-token table naming the class and the owning phase for each of the 9 unresolved tokens. **Two enumerated caveats travel with it.** `.spec.ts` may be counted RESOLVED by Phase 2's suffix stage rather than filtered by Phase 1 in any tree containing files ending `.spec.ts` — an acceptable outcome, since both paths keep it out of `unresolved`, which is all its row requires. And two of the four RC4/RC2 tokens (`index.ts` and `.spec.ts`) were INFERRED from the stack and never verified on disk, so a miss there is re-classified, never explained away — **the run measures the real number, it does not confirm a predicted one.** If it passes, adding that token table to `REGRESSION-ANCHORS.md` is part of the phase; **no anchor exists yet.** **Precedent: plan 34** (a false-positive fix on a mechanical checker applied to inputs it was never designed for) supplies OQ-2's denylist-over-allowlist shape for a polyglot framework — but **its D2 deliberately does NOT transfer**: plan 34 demoted its check to advisory, and dimension 2 keeps its full 0.25 weight here, because the `ship` override already supplies the escape valve that `/devforge:verify`'s verdict lacked. **One open housekeeping item for whoever commits this:** the plan DOCUMENT quotes token values from a private-client consumer run (component filenames among them) and, unlike plans 73/74/75, carries no untracked-disposition declaration of its own — decide that disposition before committing it rather than by default.

- `81-INFERENCE-RULES-PLAN.md` — **✅ DONE (build) 2026-08-18 — Phases 0–6 complete; Phase 7 consumer e2e DEFERRED 2026-08-18 — maintainer intends to run it (NOT waived; build-verified, NOT consumer-validated; the frozen-prompt re-run stays the HARD GATE, model pinned `claude-fable-5`).** Phase 0 ratified all ten items (a)–(j) as recommended, no overrides — per-item records live inline in the plan file (D3 recorded WITH its no-baseline bound; D5 recorded WITH the FALSE-BINARY-stays-open statement; item (j)/F6 confirmed explicitly). Builds, one commit per phase: Phase 1 F4 `9e4757a` (research Step 2b `--surface` derivation standard + matrix step-1 reconcile + Honesty-bound widening); Phase 2 F1 `9a3300b` (plan Rule 5 note + architect Rule 9 identical-reachable-tuple consequence, escalation named per Rule 6); Phase 3 F2 `af16cb3` (matrix Rule 2 value-set standard; Rule 5/Rule 9 widened in place, one statement per file; worked example enriched with a value clause on review); Phase 4 F3 `59b5152` (specify Step 4.4 role-keyed §5.2 admission + construction-site citation + both bounds; add-ac interface unchanged); Phase 5 F5 `92eadfb` (OQ-3 seam DECIDED at build: ac-verifier Rule 14 appended + plan PHASE 2.5 step 2 third bullet + PHASE 1.5 `[PLAN COVERAGE:]` carve-out — same file, file set unchanged); Phase 5b F6 `fe7329b` (grill PHASE 5 Q2 YES-arm single-bullet widening — handoffs mandatory trace inputs, transcription standard, rationale names the introducing artifact — plus a PHASE-5 opening input pointer; stage-token enumerations and references/ byte-unchanged). Tripwire held in every phase: zero Python, zero schema fields, zero helper verbs, zero new check numbers AND zero unnumbered hard-fail validators. `src/CLAUDE.md` deliberately untouched (no-op recorded: no user-visible command contract changed — the `/devforge:grill` catalog entry describes seed routing, which F6 did not change). Phase 6 reconciled: this entry + the `CLAUDE.md` one-liner, the plan-77 cross-reference amendment, `FINDINGS.md` findings 1 + 4 amendments, `CHANGELOG.md`. The pre-ratification entry text below is retained as history — its NOT-STARTED claims are superseded by this addendum, not deleted: **NOT STARTED — awaiting Phase-0 ratification; D1–D6 and OQ-1–OQ-3 are all unanswered.** Created 2026-08-17 on `develop-2.0-init`. **Subject: inference rules over facts the pipeline already computes — NOT another verification layer.** Origin: a frozen brownfield ticket (remove a small set of filters from one search surface) run 20 times across 6 spec-driven-development frameworks. **v2 produced the best run in the set** — its plan-77 emission matrix named the coupled call site 16 of 20 runs never named, its Coupling decision point surfaced the hidden second UI surface by name unprompted, its spec wrote the only acceptance criterion in the benchmark requiring both entry points to change, and its architect's rejected-alternative forcing step derived the decisive argument-level fact correctly — **and it still FAILED the handling probe**: the hidden surface (a modal reaching the same shared query-filter builder as the tab the ticket named) kept the removed filters, because the implementation added a discriminator parameter instead of changing the predicate inside the shared builder. **The causal chain is seven steps and only step 1 is a wrong input:** `/devforge:research` labelled the coupled caller's surface with the name of a lane it RESEMBLED → `/devforge:specify` wrote a preservation AC ("AC-2") protecting that mislabelled lane, over a state no reachable construction site produces → `/devforge:spec-check` returned CONSISTENT 12/12 **correctly** (an unfalsifiable AC cannot conflict with anything; Z3 sat on both quorum passes) → architect Rule 5 **correctly** derived that the two supposed lanes can present a byte-identical argument tuple to the shared builder → **the inference from that fact was wrong** ("identical tuple, opposite outputs ⇒ intrinsic derivation impossible ⇒ add a discriminator", where the correct reading of an identical REACHABLE tuple plus opposite required outputs is that the two lanes are ONE lane and the ACs conflict — a product question to escalate) → the plan's narrowing answer marked the coupled caller out of scope citing the phantom AC → the plan discharged the both-surfaces AC as "holds by construction — no file in that layer changed", file-layer reasoning discharging a behavioral claim. **One mislabel at intake survived a formal consistency proof and a correct argument-level analysis and emerged as the operative design constraint.** The benchmark's own null results (artifact volume 24×, test volume 14×, review depth from none to mutation-tested — all zero effect) are why the fix must be inference rules and never new layers: **every verification layer validates against the spec's model of the code, and more of it hardens whichever model it is given.** **Five fixes, instruction-only, each attached to a forcing step that already fires (D1: zero Python, zero schema fields, zero helper verbs, zero new check numbers AND zero new unnumbered hard-fail validators — plan 75's tripwire, both halves).** **F4 (root)** — a caller's surface label must be DERIVED from its construction sites, never taken from a lane name it resembles (`src/commands/research/main.md:505`–`:532` Step 2b, the plan-69 WI-E block; **plan 77's finding A4 is a SIBLING here, NOT a predecessor** — A4 constrains the unconstrained free-text **justification** on a `scope: out` exclusion and never mentions `--surface`, and its scenario is not this run's failure mode, since the coupled caller was classified `scope: in`; F4 constrains the **surface label**, set unconditionally whether the caller is in scope or out. Two different fields of the same `classify-caller-scope` call, so F4 is a gap plan 77 did not name). **F1** — the identical-tuple rule at `src/commands/plan/main.md:443` (Rule 5) + `src/agents/architect.md:153` (Rule 9's Rejected-alternative checkability step, **whose own worked example is the run's correctly-derived fact** — the rule produces the fact and says nothing about what follows from it): identical reachable tuple ⇒ same lane by default; identical reachable tuple + opposite required outputs ⇒ an AC CONFLICT escalated as a product question; a discriminator is admissible only after the sites are shown genuinely distinct, and "an AC says one must not change" is NOT that showing. **F2** — value sets, not expressions, in matrix cells and Rule 5 rejections; **⚠ it closes NO accounting hole and must not be written as though it does** — `src/commands/research/main.md:1158` already forces a `varies` cell to verdict `affected` (and already requires the Note to state which values were considered), and `src/commands/plan/main.md:370` + the `:539` PHASE-2.5 backstop already force every `affected` row to be accounted for; the run's row WAS flagged and WAS accounted for, with a sentence licensed by the phantom AC, so F2 removes the AMBIGUITY that made a wrong accounting sentence look plausible. **Do not ban `varies`** (deliberately permitted for dispatch-blocked sites) and do not re-add its considered-values requirement; the real delta is that neither `:443` nor the architect step requires each named argument's reachable VALUE SET rather than an expression standing for it. **F3** — preservation-AC admission (a cited, traced construction site before an AC enters as behavior preservation); **⚠ `src/commands/specify/main.md:619` already carries plan 77 R3's directive in full, naming the "we did not intend to touch that file" trap, and AC-2 was written anyway** — almost certainly because it was framed as LANE-BEHAVIOR preservation rather than VALUE presence, dodging the lexical trigger; so F3 re-keys the trigger on the AC's declared ROLE (the shipped `add-ac --subsection behavior_preservation` key → §5.2, `:637`/`:648`) and adds the reachability citation. Honest bounds carried: the plan-62 D9 parallel (a permitting case counts only when asserted reachable), static reachability is bounded by dynamic dispatch, and the role key narrows the dodge surface without closing it. **`/devforge:spec-check` stays opt-in ADVISORY per its ratified D14 — this plan proposes no change to it.** *(Reconciled 2026-08-19: plan 81 changed nothing about that command and that scope claim stands; D14 was later amended in place by plan 82's ratified D6 — run-mandatory, verdict never binding — so the clause above records the stance as of 2026-08-18, not the current one.)* **F5** — a behavioral AC cannot be discharged by file-layer reasoning; **⚠ the plan template has NO test-boundary and NO verification-strategy section** (verified — the only `test` hits are the property-test lane), so the real plan-side seam is PHASE 2.5 steps 1–3 (`:528`–`:534`), which check AC coverage against Layer Map / File Impact — i.e. the invalid discharge is written into the step meant to catch the gap; the verify-side seam (OQ-3) is `src/agents/ac-verifier.md` Rule 14 (Rules run 1–13) or `src/commands/verify/main.md:180`'s brief, decided at build. **D2 priority: F4 → F1 → F2 → F3 → F5**, defence in depth rather than a chain (F1–F3 each catch F4's failure downstream independently). **D3 — plan-77 disposition is KEEP AND AMEND and this plan is the amend:** the external benchmark effectively supplied plan 77's WAIVED measurement — the matrix is VINDICATED on discovery, the probe FAILED on handling — and under 77's retired D6 a non-flipping probe was the delete branch, but the evidence supports keep-and-amend because the discovery value is measured and the failure localizes to named inference steps. **Its honest bound, which must travel with the claim:** what ran is 77's Phase-3 ARM with **no Phase-1 baseline**, so it is an "after" with no "before" — consistent with the matrix working, NOT a demonstration that it did, and it cannot separate matrix-attributable discovery from v2's other post-v1.28 gains. Recording this **supersedes nothing in plan 77's file**; its entry above stays as-is and Phase 6 adds a short cross-reference amendment to it. **D4 — F3 takes OWNERSHIP of the file-less preservation-AC finding below** (the two AC failure shapes — pins-a-removed-value, and unreachable-subject-state — are siblings handled by one admission rule). **D5 — NO `src/constitution.md` edit; the FALSE BINARY finding stays OPEN and UNTOUCHED.** F1 is the mechanical trigger that finding has lacked, so **F1 IF ratified and IF validated at Phase 7 becomes a candidate route for it — and that entry's "no designated route" statement STANDS until then.** The run is why: Rule 5 DID consider the intrinsic form first and rejected it **on the phantom AC's authority, not on the rule's preference** — the phantom, not the preference, was decisive, so a constitution edit would not have helped. **Phase 7 acceptance test (D6, user-driven HARD GATE):** re-run the frozen prompt; **pass = the hidden surface stops emitting the removed values WITHOUT the prompt naming it and WITHOUT that surface's component file appearing in the diff** (a predicate change inside the shared builder, not a call-site sweep). **The model must be PINNED** — this run was `claude-fable-5` and all v1 benchmark runs were `claude-opus-5`, a live confound. Two cheap secondary checks are recorded as MANUAL observations and explicitly NOT gates: grep the run's artifacts for a `varies` matrix value cell lacking an enumerated value set, and for a preservation AC lacking a cited construction site. One run across five fixes attributes nothing to any single one — Phase 7 gates the outcome, it does not measure attribution. **OQ-1** (a mechanical detector for F3 later — recommend no for v1, prose-first per plan 66's Narrowing), **OQ-2** (can a prose trigger be made non-dodgeable — honest answer NO; the mitigation is the role key, recorded as mitigation and not as a solution), **OQ-3** (F5's verify-side seam). Every `main.md` / `architect.md` / `ac-verifier.md` edit in Phases 1–5 routes through instruction-author → instruction-reviewer + claude-code-guide (they ship into consumer `.claude/`). **Evidence: `81-EVIDENCE-V2-BENCHMARK-RUN.md` at repo root — carries private-client identifiers, must NEVER be committed and never quoted into any tracked file** (same disposition as the untracked plans 73/74/75 and `77-EVIDENCE-…`; plan 78's delete-at-v2-go-live route, not a scrub). It shares this plan's `81-` prefix but is EVIDENCE, not a second plan file; an `81-*` glob returns exactly these two — this plan and the evidence file (verified 2026-08-17). An unrelated plan briefly carried the same number and has been renumbered to `83-DOWNSTREAM-REENTRY-SEED-PLAN.md`; that collision no longer exists and must not be re-added from a stale index line. **Amended 2026-08-18:** **F6 added — a SIXTH fix, pre-ratification, so the plan now carries six fixes and the "Five fixes" count in the prose above is the pre-amendment shape.** **F6 — attribution traces to INTRODUCTION, not restatement:** when `/devforge:grill` PHASE 5's Q2 reaches RE-ENTER-UPSTREAM, the feature's `research-handoff.json` and `discover-handoff.json` (where present) become MANDATORY trace inputs, and where the invalidated conclusion's substance appears in an upstream handoff the introducing stage is that upstream artifact's stage — `spec` is the introducing stage only when the conclusion has no upstream source, because where the text was WRITTEN is not where the error was INTRODUCED. **Its origin is a SECOND observed incident, NOT the benchmark:** maintainer-reported 2026-08-18 from a consumer install where `/devforge:research` delivered a defective conclusion, `/devforge:grill` caught it, and the attribution settled on the transcribing stage — a conversational report whose only record is the plan's own F6 origin paragraph (that run's `grill.md` lives in the consumer install, not in this repo), so it carries weaker provenance than the benchmark and must not be merged into the seven-step chain. **Target is a single site** — one sentence in `src/commands/grill/main.md` (PHASE 5 Q2's YES arm, the "attributed to the NEAREST introducing stage" sentence, the ONLY statement of the attribution rule; the four other "nearest" occurrences plus `references/report-format.md` are stage-token enumerations and stay byte-unchanged), widened at a **new Phase 5b** that is letter-suffixed precisely so Phases 6 and 7 keep the numbers cited outside the plan file. **Severity, verified:** a spec-targeted seed for a research-introduced defect guarantees re-derivation, because the `/devforge:specify` re-entry run re-imports the same `research-handoff.json` and nothing in the seed consumer invalidates it. The `research` route already exists at `/devforge:research` Phase 0.6 and **F6 does not build it**; F6 records one unanswered residual (paraphrased transcription still mis-attributes — narrowed, not closed, in OQ-2's style); the amendment added **no new D-number and no new OQ-number**, so F6 ratifies as Phase-0 item **(j)** and a ratification recorded only against D1–D6/OQ-1–OQ-3 would leave it unratified. Two knock-ons for a future session: the plan's own D2 order is now F4 → F1 → F2 → F3 → F5 → F6, and **plan 82's line hints into plan 81 shifted and were refreshed in the same change** — `:128`→`:129` (F3's spec-check sentence), `:174`→`:199` (the non-goal bullet) and `:156`→`:181` (D4) across the root `CLAUDE.md` one-liner, `82-SPEC-CHECK-SUBJECT-RESOLUTION-MANDATORY-PLAN.md` (five sites) and the plan-82 entry in THIS file (two sites), digits only, each verified against the quoted claim it carries. **(AMENDED 2026-08-19 — the last two moved AGAIN when Phase 0's ratification records landed: `:199`→`:201` and `:181`→`:183`, refreshed across the same sites; `:129` did not move.)** **No stale hint into plan 81 remains.** Grep the quoted claims, never the digits.
