Rule 1), `src/CLAUDE.md` (chain UNBRACKETED for spec-check with grill still bracketed, the human-typed-seven sentence's descriptor, the caveat paragraph, the one-liner, the `#### /devforge:spec-check` entry, a precondition line on the `#### /devforge:plan` stub, and a **new Hard-Gates row**), `specify/main.md`'s no-seed sentence, `spec-formalizer.md`'s two disposition clauses, `storage-rules.md`'s lifecycle line, `formalization-guidance.md`'s plan-vocabulary heading leak (`(the D9 case)` → `(the permission-clash case)`), plus `FINDINGS.md` finding 4's further additive dated amendment, plan-81 reconciliation notes at three sites, `DEVELOPMENT-STATUS.md`'s agent-category line, and the `CHANGELOG.md` `[Unreleased]` entry. **Discovered by the sweep and fixed as omissions in the plan's own list** (that list was explicitly not certified exhaustive): `spec-check/main.md`'s z3 "burdens only users who invoke it" rationale, `src/devforge/storage-rules.md`'s `opt-in` lifecycle descriptor, `README.md`'s bracketed flow chain + its two `optional (opt-in)` sentences, `DEVELOPMENT-STATUS.md`'s `spec-check.md` catalog entry + its `plan.md` entry (which named no mechanical gate) + its agent-category line, plan 63's OQ-2 keep-7 reason (it QUOTES the description sentence this pass rewrote — the KEEP decision is unaffected and the 13/7 counts are unchanged, but the stated reason needed a dated note **under (a-ii) too**, which the plan had scoped to (a-i) only), plan 66's FOUR `/spec-check`-is-opt-in claims (its `[VERIFIED]` premise bullet under **Verified state**, its WI-1 trigger parenthetical, its plan-62 line under **Dependencies + related**, and its **Context for next session** trap paragraph — all four amended, none of them reviving that plan's v1 Option-A trigger or its stub-generator trap, both of which rest on the scratch-only-IR premise that is untouched and independently sufficient), and plan 86's dependency-line tense. OQ-1 was RESOLVED 2026-08-17 and ratified nothing else. **The per-item ratification records live inline in the plan file** (`**Ratified 2026-08-19:**` / `*Resolved:*` paragraphs, each with its one-sentence reason); the drafting text below each is retained as history and its pre-ratification framing is superseded by those records, not deleted. Created 2026-08-17 on `develop-2.0-init`. The plan document carries no private-client identifiers and is intended to be **committed normally**, unlike the deliberately-untracked plans 73/74/75. **Subject: two separable changes to `/devforge:spec-check` delivered by one maintainer directive** (recorded verbatim in the plan's Origin section) — (i) **subject resolution** in the `spec-formalizer`, and (ii) **mandatory run + auto-accept of the clean verdict**, which reverses plan 62's ratified D14. **Motivating incident, at the abstraction level of the plan-81 entry above and no deeper:** `/devforge:specify` wrote a preservation AC over a state no reachable construction site produces; `/devforge:spec-check` returned **CONSISTENT 12/12 — correctly**, because an unfalsifiable AC cannot conflict with anything and Z3 was `sat` on both quorum passes; the phantom AC then became the operative design constraint. **Nothing in the current design distinguishes "these ACs are mutually consistent" from "one of these ACs asserts something about a state that does not exist"** — both render `sat`, both recommend CONSISTENT, and the coverage ledger records the phantom as `formalized`, the most confident status it has. Subject resolution converts a sound proof over a phantom into a **refusal to formalize** it. **It clears plan 77's visibility bar** (*does the rule produce an artifact that is visibly wrong when the analysis wasn't done?*) on content grounds — an `UNRESOLVED SUBJECTS` section naming the AC, the search performed, and the absent construction site reads wrong on its face — **with an honest bound stated in the plan and not buried: a formalizer that cites a plausible-but-irrelevant construction site passes every mechanical check and produces a resolved AC. D3 validates that a citation EXISTS and points at real text; nothing validates that it is the RIGHT site.** **D1 — the record is per VARIABLE, not per AC** (the `variables[]` block is the co-reference anchor, plan 62 D8 — the same quantity across three ACs is one variable, resolved once), either a citation (repo-relative path + symbol/line + one line of what was found) or `unresolved` plus what was searched; a fourth `COVERAGE_STATUSES` value `unresolved_subject` joins the tuple; such an AC is reported under a dedicated `## UNRESOLVED SUBJECTS` heading as a **FORMALIZATION FAILURE** (never as a proved spec defect), is NOT formalized into constraints, and is accounted separately — **`M` stays `len(acs)`; the AC is counted in neither `N` (formalized) nor `K` (unformalizable) and the headline grows a third term `J unresolved subjects`.** Dispositions stay 3-way; `DISMISS` remains the escape when the human knows the site the search missed. **D2 — RATIFIED 2026-08-19 as recommended (both arms; preservation ACs arm-(a)-only, keyed on the `behavior_preservation` role plan 81's F3 shipped at `/devforge:specify` Step 4.4). It is the load-bearing decision and the plan argues it hard: the directive's LITERAL rule cannot survive contact with greenfield ACs.** The command runs between `/devforge:specify` and `/devforge:plan`, before any implementation, so an AC describing behavior this feature INTRODUCES has no construction site **by definition** — under the literal rule every new-behavior AC is unresolved, the clean verdict NEVER fires on a greenfield spec, and the mandatory gate degrades into ritual noise (the cry-wolf failure plan 19 and D14 both exist to prevent). Recommendation: a subject resolves via **either arm** — (a) a construction site in existing code, required for every AC presupposing an existing state, or (b) the spec's own new-behavior declaration — **with preservation ACs arm-(a)-only**, since a preservation AC resolving only via arm (b) is self-contradictory and is exactly the phantom shape. **Recorded counter: arm (b) is a self-attestation escape valve** an author or the formalizer can misroute an existing-state claim through; the role restriction covers both benchmark sibling shapes and **narrows the dodge surface without closing it** — the same honest bound plan 81's OQ-2 records for prose triggers, and it must not be reported as a solution. **D3 — the POSITIVE claim is made hard-checkable** (deterministic file check: the cited file exists and the cited symbol/text is present; a citation failing it is treated as unresolved, not trusted) while **the NEGATIVE claim stays soft**, and that asymmetry is what D4 rests on. **D4 — the resolution quorum polarity is INVERTED vs D13's unsat-core quorum, and the plan says so explicitly so a future session does not "unify" them:** resolution is an existence claim, so **resolved-in-ANY-pass = resolved** and `UNRESOLVED-SUBJECT` is reported only when ALL k passes fail (with what each searched shown, per plan 62 D4's falsifiable-human-check discipline); the unsat core keeps strict majority because a one-off contradiction must not become a recommendation. **Unifying them would convert every single-pass search miss into a reported failure** — precisely the false-positive machine D14 feared. **D5 — mandatory wiring: RATIFIED 2026-08-19 as option (a), a fail-closed preflight in `/devforge:plan`**, on the grounds that the gate belongs to the consumer stage (matching `specify_helper find-handoffs --require`, which blocks `/devforge:specify` until an intake handoff exists, exits 2 with a BLOCKED message, and states "mandatory, with no override" — plan 48 independently names it the closest forward-precondition analogue) and that the alternative (b) `/devforge:specify` tail-run **misses a hand-edited `spec.md`**, which never re-enters that command. **Two verified build costs are recorded so ratification is not blind: `plan_helper` has NO preflight verb (twelve verbs, none of them a gate) and `/devforge:plan` has NO mechanical gate at all — its setup-chain "hard gate" is PROSE reading `constitution.md` for the unpopulated sentinel — so (a) means a NEW verb plus a NEW phase block and would be the first fail-closed mechanical preflight in that command.** **The model-invocability sub-fork was RATIFIED 2026-08-19 as (a-ii): `disable-model-invocation: true` STAYS on `/devforge:spec-check`, the blocked message names the command for the USER to type, so plan 63's ratified 13/7 carve-out is NOT reopened, NO description trim is owed and this plan contributes NO count delta — the two-turn round trip through the user is the accepted cost, and "mandatory" is satisfied because the check RAN. The rejected arm is retained below as the record of what was weighed:** (a-i) flip `disable-model-invocation: true` → model-invocable (the brief's recommendation), which **reopens plan 63's ratified 13/7 carve-out for one of the seven (13/7 → 14/6) and drags a description trim**, since removing the flag puts a ~200-word description into the always-on skill listing against the ≈40-word budget plan 63's OQ-1 set; or (a-ii) keep the flag and have `/devforge:plan` name the command for the user to type, which costs nothing in plan 63's terms and is strictly slower. **"Mandatory" is satisfied by either, because what becomes mandatory is that the check RAN — not who typed it.** **Auto-accept semantics:** CLEAN = quorum-stable `consistent` AND zero unresolved subjects AND zero mechanical-validation failures → **no human gate fires**, the report is still rendered, written and WIP-committed (plan 37 discipline; PHASE 7 was already unconditional), and the run proceeds; anything else fires the existing `AskUserQuestion` gate with the plan-62 D3 dispositions unchanged and `DISMISS` surviving. **The gate checks presence + freshness ONLY, never the verdict** — a `REVISE-SPEC` report does not itself block `/devforge:plan`; acting on it rewrites `spec.md`, which invalidates the report under OQ-2's freshness predicate and re-blocks the gate. **A gate that read the verdict would be the blocking-on-a-stochastic-formalizer design D14 correctly refused; this one is not that, and that narrow reading is the entry's most load-bearing sentence.** **D6 — RATIFIED 2026-08-19 WITH the narrow reading confirmed explicitly (mandatory = the check RAN and its report is FRESH; the verdict NEVER binds), and as first-of-its-kind on the maintainer's own directive per OQ-1. The D14 reversal is a formal post-ratification amendment to plan 62, made in place** (the same mechanism that plan used for its own D9) and recording **WHY the calculus changed, not merely that it did**: original D14 equated mandatory with interrupt-on-every-run atop a stochastic formalizer, the new regime interrupts ONLY on findings, and the false-positive surface D14 feared is narrowed by four mechanisms that did not exist when it was written (D2's arm structure, D3's citation validation, D4's any-pass polarity, the surviving DISMISS escape). What does NOT change is recorded too: the human owns every non-clean verdict, the command never edits `spec.md`, D11's under-promise boundaries and D13's fixed k=2 strict-majority quorum stand. **Separability is explicit — D1–D4 and D8 ship coherently under the CURRENT opt-in stance, so a Phase-0 rejection of D6 keeps Phases 1–4 and drops 5–6; it is not a rejection of the plan.** **(DISCHARGED 2026-08-19 — D6 was NOT declined, so all six build phases stand; the separability rule is retained as the record of what a decline would have meant.)** The reversal drags a cross-reference sweep (Phase 6) across plan 62's D14 + opening, `spec-check/main.md`'s description / body / Rule 1 / frontmatter, `src/CLAUDE.md`'s bracketed chain entry + both "seven human-typed only" sentences + the catalog entries, `FINDINGS.md` finding 4 (**amend, do not delete, do not renumber, do not touch its shape note — finding 5 back-references it**), plan 81's `:129` and `:201` (digits re-verified 2026-08-19), both ledger files, and — under (a-i) only — plan 63's OQ-2 keep-7 enumeration and the 13/7 counts. **The sweep list is explicitly NOT certified exhaustive**; Phase 6 opens with a grep. **Recorded counter to D6, unresolved by the plan:** D14 was ratified *after* a design critique that specifically weighed and rejected an earlier make-it-blocking push, so reversing it on a directive rather than on new evidence rests on the same kind of reasoning the original did, and the incident is ONE run — the ratifier is asked to confirm the narrow reading explicitly, because a future session reading "D14 reversed" without it will over-generalize and will be able to cite this plan while doing so. **(The ratifier DID confirm it explicitly, 2026-08-19 — the counter itself is unresolved by the plan and stays recorded.)** **D7 — the z3 collision the directive does not address.** Plan 62 D10 keeps `install.sh` stdlib-clean and preflights `import z3` with a clean one-time pip message; **verified 2026-08-17 that `install.sh` contains zero `z3` and zero `pip install` occurrences**, so the stance is live. A mandatory gate forces the dependency on every consumer or blocks `/devforge:plan`. Options: (a) `install.sh` installs `z3-solver`, reversing the stdlib-clean stance the always-on substrate depends on; (b) degrade to a warning when z3 is absent — **REJECTED**, an escape hatch that collides with the zero-escape-hatch policy and silently un-mandatories the gate on exactly the machines that never provisioned it; (c) **RATIFIED 2026-08-19** — fail closed with the one-time install message, so plan 62 D10's stdlib-clean stance survives and `install.sh` stays untouched. (a) is recorded as the named fallback if consumer friction proves too high at Phase 7, and (b) stays REJECTED. **D8 — defence in depth with plan 81's F3, NOT supersession.** F3 (authoring-side preservation-AC admission at `/devforge:specify`, keyed on the shipped `behavior_preservation` role) refuses the phantom at the point of writing — **AMENDED 2026-08-19: F3 is SHIPPED, not proposed (plan 81 Phase 4, 2026-08-18, `59b5152`, `/devforge:specify` Step 4.4), and it is build-verified, NOT consumer-validated, since plan 81's Phase 7 e2e is deferred — so no claim here may rest on it having been observed to work**; this plan catches survivors mechanically one stage later — which matters because plan 81's own record is that the shipped `/devforge:specify` directive was present, correct, and dodged by framing. Subject resolution directly catches the unreachable-subject sibling; **the pins-a-removed-value sibling is caught by Z3 only when the formalizer binds the removal AC and the pin AC to shared variables, and code-grounded resolution makes shared binding MORE likely without guaranteeing it** — stated as an honest bound, so nothing here is written as closing `FINDINGS.md` finding 4. **Recorded collision — AMENDED 2026-08-19:** plan 81 explicitly states it *"proposes no change to `/devforge:spec-check`"* (`:129`, `:201` — digits re-verified 2026-08-19), and **that statement is now SHIPPED, ratified text: plan 81's Phases 0–6 landed 2026-08-18**, its D4 ratified (F3 OWNS `FINDINGS.md` finding 4) and finding 4 was **already amended in place 2026-08-18** by plan 81's own Phase 6. **So the collision is LIVE rather than contingent, and plan 82's Phase-6 reconciliation is UNCONDITIONAL** — it writes the ownership split unambiguously (F3 = admission at authoring; this plan = refusal at formalization) against live text, and its `FINDINGS.md` edit is a FURTHER additive dated layer on top of plan 81's, never a first amendment. **The clause plan 82 answers survives verbatim in finding 4's original text** — *"a MANDATORY refusal of this shape has no owner today"* — and its stated reason is the opt-in D14 stance that a ratified D6 changes. **⚠ OQ-1 — the plan's sharpest item — is RESOLVED 2026-08-17 by maintainer statement (*"side note - i decided to make grill mandatory"*), and the answer is NEITHER of the two the discrepancy was drafted against: the grill precedent is a FRESH maintainer decision that has landed NOWHERE in the repo.** The framing evidence is therefore not overturned; it becomes the record of what has NOT yet moved — verified 2026-08-17 and unchanged as of this writing: `/devforge:grill` carries `disable-model-invocation: true` and its Rule 1 still reads *"Opt-in, never an auto-gate"* — **RETIRED CLAUSE BY CLAUSE 2026-08-26 by plan 85's build: the `disable-model-invocation: true` clause is STILL TRUE (D6 ratified keeping the flag, so the 13/7 counts did not move), while the Rule-1 clause is now FALSE — Rule 1 reads "Mandatory to RUN, never binding in its VERDICT"**; plan 23's entry above records that the USER owns the verdict at the `/devforge:breakdown` gate; `48-REVIEW-MANDATORY-GATE-PLAN.md` — the repo's one mandatory-gate push — is SHELVED; and a repo-wide case-insensitive grep for grill within 80 characters of *mandator* returns exactly one hit, `src/CLAUDE.md` saying *"not a mandatory gate."* — **RETIRED 2026-08-26: plan 85's build removed that bullet's *"not a mandatory gate"* clause, so this observation no longer describes the tree.** **Consequence for D6: the sentence authorizing this plan's second half points at a same-day decision, not at prior art, so D6 is a first-of-its-kind decision and must be argued as one — "grill did it too" is not available to the ratifier, and a future grill-mandatory plan cannot cite this plan's unratified D6 either.** **A sibling grill-mandatory change IS intended and stays OUT of this plan's scope — that plan EXISTS as `85-GRILL-MANDATORY-AUTO-ACCEPT-PLAN.md`** (created 2026-08-17, NOT STARTED, awaiting its own Phase-0 ratification — recorded 2026-08-19), on structural grounds recorded in OQ-1: grill is soft all the way down (LLM findings filtered through the `_shared/` refutation engine, **no deterministic Z3-analog layer**), so its clean-verdict analog is the weaker *"no finding survived refutation"* with a different false-positive profile; its disposition set is **4-way** (`PROCEED` | `REVISE-PLAN` | `RE-ENTER-UPSTREAM` | `KILL`) and its seat is between `/devforge:plan` and `/devforge:breakdown`, so gate host, blocked-arm message and seed interaction are a different design rather than a re-parameterization of D5; and mandatory grill puts the full finder + refuter fan-out on every feature with no cheap deterministic core, so **that plan's Phase 0 owes a wall-clock cost line (cf. plan 70)** — OQ-3 is this plan's analogous obligation and does not transfer. **Only the auto-accept SHAPE transfers: interrupt the human only when a finding exists, render and commit the artifact either way.** It is resolved first because the sentence authorizing D5–D7 points at it. **OQ-2 — the staleness predicate: RESOLVED 2026-08-19 as the CONTENT HASH** — record a `spec.md` content hash in the report at render time and re-hash it at the gate, since **mtime is fragile across checkouts and this repo already distrusts mtime-adjacent signals** (the drift check keys on git SHAs, plan 74's memory probe on content state); counter — that couples two commands through rendered markdown, and a sibling JSON stamp is a third option at the cost of a new artifact plus a `storage-rules.md` row. **AMENDED 2026-08-19 — OQ-2 can no longer be ratified alone:** plan 85's D4 adopts the SAME predicate (content hash, not mtime) on a different, free CARRIER (`grill-state.json`, already helper-owned and already committed) and states both plans MUST ratify the predicate identically, since two gates disagreeing about what "stale" means is itself the defect — so each plan's ratification record names the other's item (plan 82's OQ-2 ↔ plan 85's D4); the carrier may differ, the predicate may not. **Plan 82 ratified FIRST (2026-08-19) and its OQ-2 record names plan 85's D4, so plan 85's own Phase 0 now owes the reciprocal naming — a plan-85 ratification that picks a different predicate reopens this one.** **OQ-3 — mandatory cost: RESOLVED 2026-08-19 — the per-subject search is bounded explicitly in the emitted instruction, the exact bound chosen at Phase 2 (where the instruction and its reference examples are written) and the observed false-UNRESOLVED rate recorded at Phase 7.** Every feature now pays k=2 formalizer passes PLUS per-subject code search on every pass, where the agent today reads only its brief; recommend an explicit per-subject bound in the emitted instruction (the same spirit as the shipped bounded-hops walk at `/devforge:research` Step 2b), with the exact bound decided where the instruction is written — **an unbounded search instruction is how wall-clock blows up, and plan 70 exists because per-command wall-clock was never measured.** Counter recorded: **the bound and the false-UNRESOLVED rate are the same dial**, and Phase 7 is the only place its setting is observed. **Phases:** 0 ratification (HARD stop, no `src/` edit) · 1 IR schema + `unresolved_subject` + mechanical citation validation (python-engineer → python-reviewer, test-first) · 2 the resolution duty across `_brief.py`'s OUTPUT CONTRACT, **`src/agents/spec-formalizer.md`** and `references/formalization-guidance.md` · 3 any-pass quorum merge + the `UNRESOLVED SUBJECTS` section + the extended coverage line + the clean predicate (+ `references/report-format.md`, which goes stale the moment the renderer changes) · 4 `main.md` auto-accept-clean arm · 5 the mandatory wiring per D5 · 6 the D14 amendment + the full sweep · 7 consumer e2e, **user-driven HARD GATE**, a known-answer anchor with five scored cases (phantom ⇒ UNRESOLVED-SUBJECT and no auto-accept · clean ⇒ auto-accept with no human gate · greenfield ⇒ resolves via arm (b) and does not false-fail · the gate blocks/passes/re-blocks on freshness · the z3-absent path). **Verified build facts the plan records so a build session does not rediscover them:** `COVERAGE_STATUSES` is a three-tuple at `ir_schema.py:49`; **`_SKIPPED_STATUSES` exists in TWO copies** (`_consume.py:314`, `_report.py:72`); **the agreement rule at `_consume.py:445`–`:450` branches only on `"formalized"` and `_SKIPPED_STATUSES`, so a fourth status carrying a constraint passes validation today with no error**; the coverage headline at `_report.py:330`–`:333` counts `n` as `formalized` and `k` as the skips, **so a fourth status silently vanishes from the ledger** — i.e. a fourth coverage status is NOT additive, it is three edit sites; and **the formalizer agent is already tool-capable of searching the consumer codebase (`tools: Read, Grep, Glob`, `spec-formalizer.md:4`) but is currently INSTRUCTED NOT TO — *"Produce the IR from that input alone"* (`:26`) — so the change is an instruction change with NO tools change, and that agent file is a mandatory Phase-2 target rather than an optional one.** Claude Code frontmatter semantics were verified against current docs (`https://code.claude.com/docs/en/slash-commands`, fetched 2026-08-17, now serving the merged *Extend Claude with skills* page — *"Custom commands have been merged into skills"*): `disable-model-invocation` *"Set to `true` to prevent Claude from automatically loading this skill … Default: `false`"*, and `description` is what Claude uses to decide when to apply the skill, truncated at 1,536 characters in the listing — which is why (a-i) drags a description trim. **Non-goals:** making `/devforge:grill` mandatory (OQ-1 records the maintainer's 2026-08-17 decision to do it and why it cannot ride along; that work is `85-GRILL-MANDATORY-AUTO-ACCEPT-PLAN.md`'s subject and no phase here builds it); **re-opening or amending** any of plan 81's F1–F6 (**AMENDED 2026-08-19: all six SHIPPED 2026-08-18, so the non-goal is to leave them alone rather than not to build them**; the two plans stay independent and neither is a precondition for the other, and **file overlap is not site overlap** — plan 82's Phase 5 adds a NEW PHASE-0a block to `src/commands/plan/main.md`, a file plan 81 edited at other sites); vacuity or gap detection (plan 62 D7 — an unresolved subject is a FORMALIZATION failure, not a vacuity proof; do not let the words converge); a second adversarial-LLM formalization verifier (plan 62 D8, still v2 — D3's file check is deliberately not a second opinion); growing the disposition set past three; making the `/devforge:plan` gate read the verdict; and touching plan 71's dead-code chain or plan 77's matrix accounting. **Discovered while drafting, NOT owned by this plan and not fixed:** `48-REVIEW-MANDATORY-GATE-PLAN.md:8` still states *"Every forge command sets `disable-model-invocation: true`"*, which plan 63 falsified — verified 2026-08-17 that seven of twenty command files carry the flag. Route it separately; no phase in plan 82 touches that file. **As of 2026-09-03 (plan 93): the flag is gone from `/devforge:spec-check` — the carve-out WAS reopened; the gate half of D5(a-ii) is untouched.**

- `83-DOWNSTREAM-REENTRY-SEED-PLAN.md` — **✅ DONE (build) 2026-08-20 (Phases 0–5; commits `c453929` Phase 0 ratification / `0734697` Phases 1+4 / `70db52b` Phase 2 / `2ca7b34` Phase 3, plus the Phase-5 docs commit this entry rides in). Phase 6 consumer e2e DEFERRED 2026-08-20 — user-driven HARD GATE, NOT run (build-verified, NOT consumer-validated; the known-answer anchor is the plan's own Phase-6 procedure — a scope-change bounce that TODAY ends the turn and leaves nothing behind must write the seed, and `/devforge:specify` must announce re-entry naming the seed's `source` + `feature` and how the run addresses `must_satisfy`).** Created 2026-08-17; **numbered 83, renumbered from `81-` — the old `81-DOWNSTREAM-*` name no longer exists** (the collision plan 81's entry below records as resolved). `/devforge:fix` becomes the THIRD `ReEntrySeed` producer, and the first downstream of `/devforge:plan`: its PHASE-1 scope-change bounce already named WHICH item and WHY and then discarded that diagnosis, so the re-run re-derived it. **Phase 0 ratified every item — D1–D6 + OQ-1–OQ-6 — on 2026-08-20**, most as recommended, two divergences called out in the plan's own status line: **D1** = one new source token `"fix"`, `/devforge:fix` the SOLE producer (the counter-argument is ACCEPTED, not answered — a plan proven wrong mid-`/devforge:implement` has no route to `/devforge:fix` until the tasks drain, so this covers the diagnosis moment and misses the discovery moment); **D2** = the `spec` and `plan` routes only, **NO `breakdown` target stage** (its real cost is a new consumer block + its reachability gate + a three-way triage discriminator that does not exist — the PHASE-1 triage is a flat binary — and shipping the enum member without it would orphan the target stage), and ⚠ **the `plan` route ships UN-EXERCISED this build**: the widened glob is deliberate consumer future-proofing, but the flat-binary bounce emits `target_stage="spec"` only, so no producer reaches that route and Phase 6 does not validate it; **D3** = verdict-gated three-arm `AskUserQuestion` at the bounce (`re-enter specify` recommended / `drop and re-run` / `stop`; no authored "Other" — the tool injects it), the seed written ONLY on the matching pick, because `/devforge:specify` treats a seed as a binding directive and an unratified one is the orphan plan 39 exists to prevent; **D4** = `specs/[feature]/fix-seed.json` (the `<source>-seed.json` convention — what makes `/devforge:specify`'s two glob call-sites see it with zero code change, and what mechanically FORCES OQ-4's option (i)); **D5** = a new `_fix/_seed.py` + a `write-seed` verb through `_fix/_cli.py`'s documented extension point (helper-owns-shape, so an empty required field exits 2 instead of shipping an invalid directive; the thin-caller asymmetry is ACCEPTED as recorded, not refuted); **D6** = no `scope_grade` field — `target_stage` carries the gradation. OQ answers: **OQ-1** the bounce still ends the turn after writing (naming the four-command cycle; `/devforge:fix` never runs `/devforge:specify` itself); **OQ-2** `cycle_count` = 1 unless this run itself re-entered from a prior seed, and NO cap logic; **OQ-3** de-enumerate the consumer prose (read the `source` field) with **exactly ONE illustrative value list kept**, at `specify/main.md`'s `source`-field bullet; **OQ-4**'s three sub-cases — the `conversational (in-window user report; no report file)` provenance literal, the evidence-quoting rule for a written finding (`invalidating_evidence` MUST quote that finding's own `evidence` string plus the one-line classification reason; the bare classification judgment is permitted ONLY for the conversational case), and option **(i)** ONE seed for a multi-item bounce with per-item reasoning in `carried_findings`, forced by D4's fixed filename rather than preferred; **OQ-5** no — `/devforge:review` / `/devforge:verify` do NOT become producers; **OQ-6** no producer-side union of a sibling seed's `carried_findings` (the cross-cycle compounding is left unaddressed and recorded as such). Two deferrals recorded, neither fixed: the shared `--feature` argparse asymmetry across the `_fix` / `_spec_check` CLIs (error-message SHAPE only — functionally exit-2 correct — deliberately NOT fixed, since touching `_spec_check` is a plan non-goal), and the project-wide `/devforge:plan` PHASE 0a.7 glob's cross-feature misbinding window (a pre-existing divergence from `/devforge:specify`'s deliberately dir-scoped glob, now recorded in that block rather than repaired). **Plan-85 coordination rule, still live: whichever of plans 83 and 85 ships SECOND reads the LIVE `specify/main.md:154`-area opt-in sentence and re-derives its rewrite from what it finds — never a pre-computed one.** Plan 83 shipped first, so plan 85 owes that live read. Plan 82's OQ-1 asserts in three tracked files that this plan's Phase 0 owes it, so a Phase 0 that closes without it makes all three false.** The cost: full `devils-advocate` dispatch with a three-ring blast-radius traversal and self-gated web verification, a grounding-gate pass, a per-refuter fan-out and an orchestrator classification, on every feature, **with no cheap deterministic core to absorb the common case** — and **no per-command grill number exists anywhere in this repo**, because plan 70 built the profiler but deferred its Phase-2 real-run diagnosis to post-release; what IS on record is plan 70's *"~2h wall-clock"* for the whole pre-implement chain **with grill OPTIONAL**. the plans ratify and ship INDEPENDENTLY, coupled at exactly two points (the plan-63 counts and the staleness predicate), and the combined per-feature cost is the SUM — recorded once here so nobody meets it as a surprise at e2e.** Counter: two mandatory gates in a five-command chain is a pipeline POLICY decision, and ratifying them separately is how a policy gets made without anyone deciding it — **decide the policy question once, explicitly.** **OQ-1** whether `/devforge:breakdown` auto-invokes grill or names it (mirrors plan 82's a-i/a-ii; **consistency with whatever plan 82 ratifies is a stated dependency, not a preference**; recommend naming it, per the `find-handoffs --require` shape). **OQ-2** the three known-answer e2e anchors (planted defect ⇒ gate fires and PHASE 7 runs · sound plan ⇒ auto-accept with PHASE 7's gate never firing AND `$WORKDIR` gone · `plan.md` edited after the grill ⇒ freshness conjunct fails, passes again after a re-grill), with a sub-question on whether case 1 should use a defect the adversary is KNOWN to catch (recommended — this plan changes WHEN grill runs, not WHAT it finds, so an adversary-variance failure would be uninterpretable). **OQ-3** whether KILL needs distinct gate handling — **recommend OUT OF SCOPE on D3's spine, with the consequence stated not buried: under this plan a KILLed design does NOT block `/devforge:breakdown`.** **Phases:** 0 ratification (HARD stop, no `src/` edit, MUST carry the D7 cost line) · 1 the freshness stamp + the helper-exposed clean predicate (python-engineer → python-reviewer, test-first) · 2 the `breakdown_helper` gate verb + the entry-side wiring · 3 grill `main.md` auto-accept rewiring incl. the PHASE-7 leading no-entry clause · 4 the Rule-1 amendment + the full sweep (instruction-author → instruction-reviewer + claude-code-guide; the frontmatter flip IS Claude-Code-integration surface under D6's other arm) · 5 consumer e2e, **user-driven HARD GATE**, OQ-2's anchors with wall-clock recorded separately for the clean and the finding-bearing run. **Non-goals:** reviving plan 48 / `/devforge:review`-mandatory (**SHELVED with a named revival trigger — an OBSERVED review skip — and this plan is NOT that trigger; a future session must not cite this plan as plan 48's precedent**), a grill quorum, ANY change to `/devforge:spec-check` (that is plan 82), changing grill's finder/refuter internals (**this plan changes WHEN grill runs and WHO it interrupts, never WHAT it finds**), making the gate read the disposition, growing the 4-way disposition set or the four-bucket partition, and touching consumer Claude Code skill-listing settings. **Claude Code frontmatter semantics re-verified 2026-08-17 against `https://code.claude.com/docs/en/slash-commands`** (now serving the merged *Extend Claude with skills* page): `disable-model-invocation` *"Set to `true` to prevent Claude from automatically loading this skill … Also prevents the skill from being preloaded into subagents … Default: `false`"*, the visibility table's *"Description not in context"* vs *"Description always in context"*, `description`+`when_to_use` truncated at 1,536 characters, all fields optional — **plus the fact plan 82 did not record and which sharpens D6: the listing budget scales at 1% of the context window and overflow drops the least-invoked skills' descriptions first.** **Discovered while drafting, NOT owned by this plan and not fixed:** (1) repo-root `CLAUDE.md`'s plan-37 index line still says *"Phase 6 (`/grill`) done-in-tree UNCOMMITTED"*, but the session-start `git status` snapshot listed no `src/commands/grill/` or `src/devforge/lib/_grill/` entry — **appears stale; item (3) stands.** Repo-root `CLAUDE.md`'s plan-37 index line no longer says *"done-in-tree UNCOMMITTED"*: the maintainer closed plan 37 on 2026-08-18 (*"plan 37 delivered"*), a live `git status` the same day reported `src/commands/grill/` and `src/devforge/lib/_grill/` clean, and both that plan's `CLAUDE.md` index line and its entry in this ledger were updated to match. `84-ARCHITECT-CONSULT-ACCUMULATION-PLAN.md` was appended to `CLAUDE.md`'s no-ledger-entry list the same day, so that list now carries four names. **Neither correction was made by this plan or by any of its phases** — the routing recorded in items (1) and (2) is what happened. **AMENDED 2026-08-23 — the plan document was reconciled in place against plans 81, 82 and 83, all of which shipped after its 2026-08-17 drafting; it remains NOT STARTED and the pass RATIFIED NOTHING (every D/OQ keeps its `*(OPEN…)*` marker, and the plan file's own dated notes are the authority — this clause is a pointer, not a substitute).** **Two decisions became externally CONSTRAINED rather than open:** plan 82's ratified **OQ-2** settled the staleness predicate on the **content hash**, which its own records ratify as identical to **D4** (carrier differs, predicate does not — D4 now owes only confirmation plus the reciprocal naming), and its ratified **D5(a-ii)** (fail-closed `/devforge:plan` PHASE 0a.8 preflight via `plan_helper verify-spec-check`, `disable-model-invocation: true` KEPT, **NO 13/7 count delta** so the 15/5 scenario is counterfactual) constrains **OQ-1** by that OQ's own *"stated dependency"* clause, arms **D6**'s keep-the-flag recommendation, gives **Phase 2** a live primary model, and — the gate being LIVE — makes this plan the SECOND to ratify, so **D8**'s closing rule now binds its Phase 0. **Four staleness corrections are recorded in place:** the inherited **plan-83 coordination debt** (re-read `src/commands/specify/main.md:154` live) is **DISCHARGED as a verified NO-OP**, plan 83 having already stripped every grill claim from that sentence; - `86-FOWLER-REFACTORING-GAPS-PLAN.md` — **✅ DONE (build) 2026-08-23 (Phases 0–6; commits `88832f3` Phase 0 ratification / `d6f51be` Phase 1 / `aa03419` Phase 4 / `e0fb8e4` Phase 2 / `6b46678` Phase 3 / `e249898` Phase 5, plus the Phase-6 docs commit this entry rides in — Phase 4 landed third because the five fixes are independent, not because any ordering was abandoned). Build-verified, NOT consumer-validated.** Created 2026-08-18 on `develop-2.0-init`; **Phase 0 ratified D2–D5 and OQ-1 on 2026-08-23**, D1 and Phase 1 having been PRE-RATIFIED by maintainer directive 2026-08-18 (*"i want close this gap"*) and OQ-2 RESOLVED 2026-08-18 from the code. The plan document carries no private-client identifiers and is **committed normally**, unlike the deliberately-untracked plans 73/74/75 — its `## Identifier constraint` records that the origin incident's consumer function names, feature slug and domain nouns were deliberately ABSENT at drafting and must not be added by a later amendment; the incident is carried at the abstraction level *"one task copied a sibling builder function into a second builder differing only in one call option"*, which is the entire shape the five findings rest on. **Subject: five instruction-only closures of the gaps a Fowler-grounded evaluation of this framework's refactoring discipline found** — refactoring.com's definition plus *Refactoring* 2nd ed.'s two hats (Beck), self-testing code as a precondition, the Rule of Three, the code-smells catalog and preparatory refactoring. **⚠ The evaluation's finding was NOT that the framework is weak, and the strong half is recorded first so the five read as the REMAINDER:** behavior preservation is already first-class (`refactor(scope):` = *"behavior-preserving restructuring"*, the `refactor` spec type, `record-constraint --kind not_break` rendering *"Must not break"*, plan 77's emission matrix), small steps have per-task verify gates, **the Rule of Three is in the constitution verbatim** (*"2 occurrences are fine — don't abstract prematurely. Wait for the third."*, `src/constitution.md:111`), the Long Function guideline exists (~40 lines, `:92`) as does Speculative Generality in the KISS bullets, and duplication IS hunted cross-task at `/devforge:review` and codebase-wide at `/devforge:audit`. **The motivating incident, and the inversion trap it still sets:** in a consumer install one task duplicated an existing builder into a second builder verbatim except one call option — **that duplication was PLAN-DECLARED, justified and CORRECT** (a Key Design Decision carried the decoupling rationale, the spec named the risk, the plan recorded it as an accepted DRY tension). **The gap was its UNDECLARED twin**, which was claimed by no per-task layer and is ruled out of scope at `/devforge:review` by construction — and **a future session that reads the incident as "the framework allowed a bad duplication" has inverted it and will "strengthen" F1 into a rule that fails Phase 7's case 2.** **⚠ The `/devforge:review` preamble asserted the very coverage F1 showed was incomplete** — *"Those reviewers already had their shot at every line inside any single task"* — while ruling the single-task case OUT OF SCOPE; **F1 is what makes that assertion accurate, and the out-of-scope rule must NOT be relaxed to compensate.** **WHAT SHIPPED, per fix.** **F1 (`d6f51be`) — `src/agents/code-reviewer.md` check 8 became two named arms.** It had been scoped to newly CREATED files and closed with *"Skip files that only edit existing modules"*; it now reads **File-level** (unchanged; `DUPLICATE` stays **Critical**) and **Function-level** — for each newly added function inside a file the changeset only MODIFIES, a verbatim or near-verbatim copy of ONE existing sibling differing only in literals or a single argument is a **duplicate at High**, and the closing sentence now says the file-level arm skips edit-only files while the function-level arm covers them. **The declare-and-justify → `INTENTIONAL_PARALLEL` path is PRESERVED and load-bearing.** The tier choice is recorded honestly: `High` sits inside the fleet-wide `Critical / High / Medium / Info` enum so no vocabulary was introduced, but `merge-review-panel` branches on the `### Verdict:` token and not on per-finding severity, **so the tier is communicative only.** The arm states in the shipped text that the finding is one sibling copied, not a count of repeated logic — the constitution's DRY rule still owns when a recurring pattern earns an abstraction. **Nine numbered checks stayed nine here; nothing was renumbered.** **F2 (`e0fb8e4`) — the two-hats rule at three sites, nine checks → ten.** `src/constitution.md` §3.6 gained a titled `**Two-hats (separating behavior change from restructuring):**` block **appended AFTER Narrowing at the end of the section**; `src/commands/breakdown/main.md` PHASE 3 gained a `### Two-hats partition (mixed behavior-change + restructuring tasks)` subsection whose partition rides the free-form `## Change Details`; and `src/agents/code-reviewer.md` gained appended check **10**, with checks 1–9 keeping their numbers. A MIXED unit partitions its touched functions and files into **behavior-changing** and **behavior-preserving**, and every preserving surface carries a preservation postcondition in `### Produces` — *"A surface called behavior-preserving that states no such postcondition is asserted, not declared."* **D2's landing site was FORCED, not preferred:** `_UNIVERSAL_SECTIONS` is a CLOSED literal tuple, so a new numbered constitution subsection would have been Python; the header had to be a bold title alone on its line with the colon inside the bold markers, or the §3.6 splitter drops the block **SILENTLY**; and appending at the END was required because the `*Backed by*` paragraph parses as part of **KISS's** body, so a block wedged above it would capture it. Likewise **F2's task-side landing site was forced** — `**Property targets**:` / `**Dead code removal**:` are flag-gated in `render_task_file`, so a third header field would also have been Python. **OQ-1 ratified MIXED TASKS ONLY on an objective two-conjunct trigger** (a Files-table `Modify` row touching an existing function the task does not delete, AND a `**Spec criteria**:` AC whose observable behavior this task changes); the every-task-with-a-degenerate-field fallback was DECLINED. Both conjuncts are required, and the shipped constitution text says so outright — *"there is no degenerate partition to write down."* **Check 10 also carries an existence arm, ratified as a scope amendment during the build:** a declared partition contradicted by the diff is **High** (the diff contradicts the task's own contract), and both conjuncts visible in the task file with NO partition written is **Medium** — an authoring omission, deliberately weaker — with the finding required to name the Files-table row and the AC it read. Check 10 must never invent partition content or infer which surfaces were meant to preserve behavior. **F3 (`6b46678`) — the regression-net declaration, and the two bounds that ship WITH it.** `src/commands/breakdown/main.md` PHASE 1's existing-codebase branch gained a **net-first ordering** paragraph (a task writing the covering test is ordered AHEAD of the restructuring task, the edge being an ordinary depends-on edge validated by Phase 2 like any other) and PHASE 3 gained `### Regression-net declaration (restructuring tasks over untested code)`. **D3 is the load-bearing decision: DECLARE-ALWAYS, never an OR.** The obvious phrasing (*"the net precedes, OR the window is declared"*) is an escape hatch the zero-escape-hatch policy forbids by name, so the obligation is **declare** and only the CONTENT varies — `Regression net: precedes — task NNN` or `Regression net: window accepted — <reason>` — with net-first the stated default. **⚠ Both honest bounds are IN the emitted text and must not be overstated by any later record: (1) NOTHING CHECKS the declaration** — no gate, no validator, and the second form is a short self-attestation; the rule does not prevent the uncovered window, it makes the window NAMED at decomposition instead of discovered by a reviewer afterwards, which clears plan 77's visibility bar without being enforcement. **(2) The trigger rests on a BELIEF about coverage, not a measurement** — the shipped PHASE 1 text says so in its own words (*"no phase of `/devforge:breakdown` runs a coverage tool, so it is what this analysis believes about coverage, never a measurement"*), because the alternative is a coverage-tool invocation, which is a mechanical gate, which is out of bounds. **F4 (`aa03419`) — six named smells, zero enum growth.** `src/commands/audit/references/best-practices-checklist.md` gained a `Responsibility placement & data shape` section: Feature Envy → `system_design`; Data Clumps / Primitive Obsession / Message Chains → `best_practice`; Shotgun Surgery and Divergent Change → `system_design` **or** `duplication` per the finding's evidence, each carrying a per-smell "which one and why" rule modelled on the sibling performance section's convention. **The `Category` enum did NOT grow**, every entry demands quoted code evidence in the form the section's existing bullets use, and **there is no smell-specific confidence carve-out** — smell findings enter plan 19's refutation stage unchanged and the file's existing Judgment rule already forces subjective findings to `Likely` / `Speculative`, so plan 19's precision does not regress. `src/commands/audit/main.md`'s reference-file enumeration now names responsibility-placement/data-shape too, so the description matches the section list. **D4 DECLINED the three-smell trim** (Feature Envy, Divergent Change and Shotgun Surgery are the highest-false-positive three): all six shipped, and **Phase 7's dismissal count is the only trim evidence that will ever exist** — a later session trimming without that number is trimming on nothing. **F5 (`e249898`) — the preparatory-refactoring lane at three sites, two of which add no mechanism.** `src/commands/plan/main.md`'s architect sub-question 5 gained a permissive (**MAY**) lane: when the minimal change is awkward because the existing structure resists it, the architect may record a **preparatory restructuring** as a Key Design Decisions row like any other, sequenced BEFORE the AC-satisfying decisions, re-shaping structure WITHOUT changing observable behavior, carried by its own `### File Impact` rows and carrying its own preservation contract in that decision's `Why` column. It is explicitly permissive and never obligatory, and it records a DECISION, never a task — `/devforge:breakdown` decomposes it. **`src/constitution.md` §6.1 gained ONE appended sentence and `src/agents/architect.md` Rule 9 ONE inserted sentence, and BOTH exist only to remove a contradiction the lane would otherwise create.** §6.1's wording is absolute (*"Do not refactor, improve, or 'clean up' code outside the scope of the current task"*), so a reviewer reading it against a planned preparatory task had textual grounds to call the task a violation; and Rule 9 declares the minimal-scope mandate SOVEREIGN, so without the third site — **a scope amendment ratified at Phase 0, not in the plan as drafted** — the architect could refuse the guidance it had just been given. **The plan-document condition is the load-bearing clause, and it is objective and externally checkable**: the unit is recorded in `plan.md` or it is not. **Both sentences state the negative half** — restructuring not written into `plan.md` before the work starts stays banned — and **the opportunistic-refactoring ban is KEPT**: §4.2's *"Never modify code outside your task scope."* bullet took no carve-out, so the entire F5 condition lives in §6.1 and nowhere else. Rule 9's heading, its number and its six bold forcing steps were left untouched by this plan — the inserted sentence is the whole edit. **A purely preparatory decision changes no observable behavior, so it FAILS F2's second conjunct — the Two-hats partition and check 10 do NOT apply to it at all**, and the shipped `plan/main.md` text says exactly that rather than claiming a trivial pass, because a reader who believes a check is watching when none is has the false confidence this plan exists to remove; the same paragraph routes the reader downstream to F3's rule, which DOES attach to the restructure-only task `/devforge:breakdown` derives. **ALL FIVE ARE INSTRUCTION-ONLY: zero Python, zero validators, zero new PHASE-3.5 gate numbers, zero mechanical gates — plan 75's tripwire, both halves.** The terminology carve-out the plan carried in its own Non-goals held: **"check number" means the PHASE-3.5 `verify-*` gate sequence, NOT `code-reviewer.md`'s prose checklist, which Phase 2 legitimately grew from nine items to ten.** **OQ-2 (resolved from the code, and unchanged by the build) — F1's arm rides into `/devforge:fix` with no `/devforge:fix` edit**, since its PHASE 4 runs the panel *"EXACTLY as `/devforge:implement` PHASE 6"* and dispatches by `subagent_type` against the single repo-wide `code-reviewer.md`. Two more consumers ride along and both are fine: `/devforge:audit` loads it as a Batch-A hunter, and `/devforge:review` loads it as a Batch-A finder where the injected anti-relitigation preamble scopes the per-task arm OUT — **so F1 did NOT widen `/devforge:review`**, and `emergent-issue-checklist.md` was left byte-unchanged to keep that true. **⚠ CONSUMER-INSTALL DRIFT IS EXPECTED, BY DESIGN AND NOT A REGRESSION.** Canonical §3.6 gained a rule, so `constitute_helper verify-universal-defaults` will report DRIFT/MISSING against any install whose `.devforge/constitute.json` predates this build, and the WARN-only update-time drift check (plan 44) will fire. **Back-porting into shipped installs is an explicit NON-GOAL** — consumers receive all five through `install.sh` / `update.sh`. **Phase 6 (this entry's commit) — sweep + records.** `CHANGELOG.md` gained one `feat(refactoring):` entry under the existing `## [Unreleased]` → `### Changed`; the `CLAUDE.md` index one-liner and this entry moved off the not-started status; **Phase 1 had already amended plan 05's `CLAUDE.md` index line in place** (an `EXTENDED 2026-08-23 by plan 86's F1` clause, which also records that plan 05's own DOCUMENT is not edited and still describes the file-only scope) while leaving this file's plan-05 entry un-mirrored — **a ledger-sync gap this file's own header forbids, closed at Phase 6 by the matching `AMENDED 2026-08-23` clause on that entry above**; and the check-only pass on `src/CLAUDE.md`'s *"don't repeat logic 3+ times"* Key Rule confirmed the **no-op — recorded here as DELIBERATE** so a later session does not "harmonize" that rule into one against two occurrences, which would contradict the constitution's Rule of Three. **⚠ PHASE 7 — CONSUMER OBSERVATION, USER-DRIVEN HARD GATE, NOT RUN.** Five known-answer anchors: undeclared clone ⇒ `DUPLICATE` at High and the panel does not go clean · declared clone ⇒ `INTENTIONAL_PARALLEL` and PASSES (**the motivating incident replayed; a failure here means the rule punishes the discipline it was written to protect**) · a genuinely new function beside same-shaped siblings ⇒ NOT flagged (plan 05's original smoke-test shape at function scope) · the F2 mixed-surface case · and an `/devforge:audit` run recording **how many smell findings survived refutation as a NUMBER**, since that is D4's only trim evidence. **Cases 1 and 3 are scored as a PAIR** — a rule that catches 1 by flagging everything fails 3 — and **Phase 7 is the ONLY place F1's false-positive rate is observed; nothing shipped in Phases 1–6 measured it, and no record here may claim otherwise.** **Non-goals, all honoured:** removing or weakening the opportunistic-refactoring ban; any Python; any change to `/devforge:constitute`'s universal-rule extraction (the closed tuple and the §3.6 splitter were read as CONSTRAINTS, never as surfaces to modify); new `Category` enum values; widening `/devforge:review`'s emergent-cross-task scope; back-porting into shipped consumer installs; **renumbering `code-reviewer`'s checks** (APPEND, never renumber — three plan documents cite the structural-integration check by a stale ordinal); changing `/devforge:audit`'s refutation stage; and turning any of the five into a gate. **Discovered while drafting, NOT owned by this plan and NOT fixed — all three verified still open at Phase 6:** (1) **`src/agents/code-reviewer.md` cites the constitution by `§`-number** (*"the constitution's No dead code rule (§3.5)"*, inside check 9, now at `:38`), which `src/agents-AUTHORING.md` forbids — the obligation this plan took was only that Phases 1 and 2 introduce no SECOND one, and a repo-wide grep confirms the file still contains **exactly one** `§`;
